## Introduction

#### What this is

The Czech Language Navigator is an overview of the key points important
in the study of Czech as a second language. It is organized in a way
that will be familiar to most students of language but it also contains
sections that go beyond grammar.

#### Core grammar sections

The first nine sections outline the core parts of speech in detail.
These are followed by sections on Word Order, Pronunciation and
Spelling. These sections focus on the things traditionally found in
grammar books. They describe the how the different parts of speech are
formed and how they are used in phrases and sentences.

#### Sections on usage

This is followed by a section that covers the different ways in which
Czech expresses itself going beyond grammar. Things like idioms, common
ways of speaking and various differences between Czech and English.
These things often fall between grammar books and dictionaries and may
be scattered across various textbooks. The Navigator tries to present
them more systematically.

#### Sections with useful background

The next three sections provide useful background about Czech to
learners and travelers. There is a section on the differences between
standard and non-standard Czech. This is followed by a section of trivia
related to Czech such as the history of the language that a student can
use to enrich their understanding of the context in which the language
is used.

The final section is an overview of the Czech grammatical system that is
taught in Czech schools. Many Czechs will be familiar with the structure
and terminology and conversations about the Czech language will be
easier.

You can find out more information about the origins of this document in
**Acknowledgements and licencing** at the end.

#### Explanation of symbols used

**Note:** Grammatical terms are explained in individual sections.

  ------------ --------------------- ----------- -----------------------
  C            consonant             \>          changes into

  V            vowel                 \+          is added

  -X           ending                X-          prefix

  -X-          infix                 =           equals/doesn\'t change

  Ø            ending is deleted     \^          same as previous
  ------------ --------------------- ----------- -----------------------

## Parts of speech: Nouns, Verbs and More

This section describes in detail the forms and functions of all the
different parts of speech in Czech:

- Nouns

- Adjectives

- Pronouns

- Numerals

- Verbs

- Adverbs

- Prepositions

- Conjunctions

At the end, you will also find a chart on How and when Czech words
change their forms and How parts of speech interact when combined. This
is the longest part of the navigator because it is what most learners
find challenging.

### Nouns

#### Gender

##### What is gender?

All Czech nouns have what is called gender. The gender of the noun is
most important when you need to decide on the form of numbers, pronouns,
and adjectives describing it. The words describing or determing the noun
must change their form to match the noun's gender. It also happens with
verbs in the past tense.

To match the noun's gender, the other words have to either add an
appropriate ending or changing an existing one. For example, *ten bílý
dům byl* vs *ta bílá kniha byla -* that^‑masculine^ white^‑masculine^
house was^‑feminine^ vs. that^‑feminine^ white^‑feminine^ book
was^‑feminine^.

English only has natural gender (used for people and male and female
animals) but nouns in most other European languages have gender. French,
Spanish and German are some of the best known. In Czech, just like in
these other languages, you for the most part have to learn which words
belong to what gender by heart.

Czech has three genders (like German and unlike French or Spanish which
only have two). They are *masculine*, *feminine*, and *neuter*. In some
ways, you can think about gender as if, for Czechs every object was
either a *man*, a *woman,* or a *child*. This doesn't really have any
real consequences, except, Czechs find it amusing when genders are
mismatched.

For instance "juice, coke, wine" (*džus*, *kola*, *víno*) are all of a
different gender (whereas in English they are all neuter). Thus,
*dobr[ý]{.underline} džus, dobr[á]{.underline} kola, dobr[é]{.underline}
víno* mean respectively "good^‑man^ juice, good^‑woman^ coke,
good^‑child^ wine." But Czechs find it funny when non-native speakers
mix up the gender. *Ha, ha, ha,* dobrá džus (good^‑woman^ juice),
*imagine that*.

To make it easier to remember that gender mostly matters for the words
that interact with the noun, in the charts in this collection, we use
the gender forms for the word *this*: **ten**, **ta**, **to**. The
sooner you start internalizing this connection, the better.

##### Summary of agreement rules!

The chart below summarizes the rules of agreement between nouns and
adjectives in singular and plural of the *Kdo? Co?* case.

+----------------+---------------------------------------------------+----------------------------------------------------------------+
|                | Singular                                          | Plural                                                         |
+================+:===========+:========================+:===========+:=========+:========================+:==========================+
| **Masculine\** |            |                         | muž        | TI       | dobř**[í]{.underline}** | muž[i]{.underline}        |
| mužský rod     |            |                         |            |          |                         |                           |
|                +------------+-------------------------+------------+----------+-------------------------+---------------------------+
|                | TEN        | dobr**[ý]{.underline}** | rohlík     | TY       | dobr**[é]{.underline}** | rohlík**[y]{.underline}** |
|                +------------+-------------------------+------------+          |                         +---------------------------+
|                |            |                         | čaj        |          |                         | čaj**[e]{.underline}**    |
+----------------+------------+-------------------------+------------+          |                         +---------------------------+
| **Feminine\**  | TA         | dobr**[á]{.underline}** | káva       |          |                         | káv**[y]{.underline}**    |
| ženský rod     |            |                         |            |          |                         |                           |
|                |            |                         +------------+          |                         +---------------------------+
|                |            |                         | rýže       |          |                         | rýž**[e]{.underline}**    |
+----------------+------------+-------------------------+------------+----------+-------------------------+---------------------------+
| **Neuter\**    | TO         | dobr**[é]{.underline}** | pivo       | TA       | dobr**[á]{.underline}** | piv**[a]{.underline}**    |
| střední rod    |            |                         |            |          |                         |                           |
|                |            |                         +------------+----------+-------------------------+---------------------------+
|                |            |                         | pití       |          |                         | pit**[í]{.underline}**    |

  ------- ------------------------------------------------------------------
  **!**   Masculine (TEN) nouns are further divided into **animate** and
          **inanimate**. This is reflected in plural agreement. In present
          day spoken Czech, the distinction is not always present but it is
          strictly required in the written form. In fact, it would even make
          sense to say that Czech has four genders.

  ------- ------------------------------------------------------------------

##### How to tell the gender of a noun

In Czech, you can tell the gender from the ending about 80% of the time.
The general rule is: *-C = masculine*, -*a = feminine*, and *-o =
neuter* (the exceptions are *feminines* ending in a soft consonant or
*e*, and *neuters* ending in *e* or *í*; there are also a few
*masculines* that end in *a* or rarely *e*).

Here are a few gender indicators to look out for.

###### There are some which are almost exception proof:

  ----------------------- --- ---------- -----------------------------------
  b, g, k, h, ch, d, r,   =   ten        rohlík (roll); oběd (lunch );
  p, n, m, l                             

  a                       =   ta         Praha, voda, kola, houska

  o                       =   to         město (city);

  í                       =   to         nádraží (train station)
  ----------------------- --- ---------- -----------------------------------

There are only a handful of *ten* nouns with *a* for an ending: *chleba*
(bread), *táta* (*dad*), and all -ist words like *komunista*,
*cyklista*, *turista*. There is one *ta* noun with *í* in *paní* (Mrs.).

###### The rest are shared by two genders about 50/50:

  ----------------------- --- ---------- ------------------ ----------------
  e                       =   ta/to      restaurace, rýže   kuře, moře

  s, t, v, c, j, č, ž, š, =   ten/ta     čaj, muž           kolej, noc, daň
  ř                                                         
  ----------------------- --- ---------- ------------------ ----------------

Czech *ten* nouns are divided into so called *hard* and *soft*
(according to the consonant they end in). The *soft* consonants share
their endings with feminine nouns while *hard* are limited to the
masculine nouns.

#### Case

##### What is case?

The word *case* refers to a set of endings that are used to indicate how
words relate to one another. For instance, who does what to whom. In
Czech, it is expressed through different endings. Nouns, pronouns,
numerals and adjectives all use different case ending to express things
like being an object (receiver) or a subject (initiator), or belonging
to something. All prepositions also require that a noun has a case
ending. Sometimes, the case ending tells you about whether you are
somewhere or you are going somewhere. Sometimes a case ending helps link
nouns together, e.g. if something belongs to something.

In the English sentence, *Petr has a son* the relationship between Petr
and his son is set by the fact that the word *Petr* precedes the verb
and the word *son* follows it. In Czech, this doesn\'t mean much,
because Czech word order is more flexible. The relatioship between Petr
and his some is captured by the case endings: *Petr\_ má
syn[a]{.underline}*. Thanks to the endings, you can also say *Syna má
Petr* and the meaning is the same.

All verbs require the words that follow them to change form (that is add
an ending). Most of the time, it is the object, but often there are
other roles such as recipient or part of something.

In traditional grammar, cases have names such as *accusative* or
*dative* that hint at the function. The names of the cases are not
important, however. What really matters, is how the case is used in the
language. What's more, Czechs will mostly not be familiar with these
names, they know the cases by their number. You can find out more in the
final section on *Grammar as Czechs Know It*.

The following sections will describe the functions of the various cases
and all the different rules for changing the forms of nouns, and other
words.

##### What are the functions of Czech cases?

Czech has six cases that describe the role of the word in a sentence. In
English, they are expressed either through word order or prepositions.
For example:

  ----------------------------- ------------------- -----------------------------
  A *friend* wrote. He is a         **subject**     Kamarád napsal. Je kamarád.
  *friend*.                                         

  A friend wrote a *letter*.        **object**      Kamarád napsal *dopis*.

  A friend *[o]{.underline}f      ***of* sth/sb**   Kamarád *[naší]{.underline}
  our family* wrote a letter.                       rodin[y]{.underline}* napsal
                                                    dopis.

  A friend of our family wrote   ***to* something   Kamarád naší rodiny napsal
  a letter *[to]{.underline}        /somebody**     dopis *Jan[ě]{.underline}*.
  Jana*.                                            

  A friend of our family wrote    **in/about/\...   Kamarád naší rodiny napsal
  a letter to Jana                   sth/sb**       dopis Janě *[v]{.underline}
  *[in]{.underline} a car*.                         aut[ě]{.underline}*.

  A friend of our family wrote   **with/by/through  Kamarád naší rodiny napsal
  a letter to Jana in a car          sth/sb**       dopis Janě v autě
  *[with]{.underline} a                             *plníc[ím]{.underline}
  fountain pen*.                                    per[em]{.underline}*.
  ----------------------------- ------------------- -----------------------------

**or**

  ----------------------------- ------------------- -----------------------------
  A *friend* is here. He is a       **subject**     Kamarád je tady. Je kamarád.
  *friend*.                                         

  I see a *friend*.                 **object**      Vidím kamarád[a]{.underline}.

  This is a story                 ***of* sth/sb**   To je příběh
  [of]{.underline} a *friend*.                      *kamarád[a]{.underline}*.

  I gave a book                   ***to* sth/sb**   Dal jsem knihu
  [to]{.underline} a *friend.*                      *kamarád[ovi]{.underline}*.

  This is a story                 **in/about/\...   To je příběh [o]{.underline}
  [about]{.underline} a              sth/sb**       *kamarád[ovi]{.underline}.*
  *friend*.                                         

  I will come                    **with/by/through  Přijdu [s]{.underline}
  [with]{.underline} a *friend*      sth/sb**       *kamarád[em]{.underline}*.
  ----------------------------- ------------------- -----------------------------

**or**

  ----------------------------- ------------------- -----------------------------
  A *book* is here. It is a         **subject**     *Kniha* je tady. Je to
  *book*.                                           *kniha*.

  I have a *book*.                  **object**      Mám *knih[u]{.underline}*.

  A page [of]{.underline} the     ***of* sth/sb**   Strana *knih[y]{.underline}*.
  *book*.                                           

  He admitted [to]{.underline}   ***to* something   Přiznal se [k]{.underline} té
  the *book*.                       /somebody**     *kni[ze]{.underline}*.

  He wrote it [in]{.underline}    **in/about/\...   Napsal to [v]{.underline} té
  the *book*.                        sth/sb**       *kni[ze]{.underline}*.

  He hit him [with]{.underline}  **with/by/through  Uhodil ho tou
  the *book*.                        sth/sb**       *knih[ou]{.underline}*.
  ----------------------------- ------------------- -----------------------------

The underlined words or endings have equivalent functions in both
languages. All the cases, except the subject, can be used with
prepositions. The 6^th^ case *in/about \...* sth/sb is only used after a
preposition.

You can ask for all the *italicized* words using question words *WHO?*
and *WHAT?* *What* is it? *What* do you have? With *whom* will you come?
In Czech, it is the same only there is a different form of *Who? What?*
for each case. Therefore the cases are named by their Czech *Who? What?*
questions, which is how Czech children learn them in school. The cases
are also numbered. In English, they are known by their Latin names. You
can see that summarized in the chart below. The cases are listed in the
same order as in the examples above.

**Note:** In most textbooks and grammar books you find 7 cases. But the
seventh case is only used for addressing people, and does not express
any relationship between things in the world. That's why it is best to
learn it separately. It is mostly confusing when listed with the other
forms and that's why it is left out of the charts and has its own
section on addressing people.

##### Summary of Czech cases

  ------------------- ---------------------- --------------- --------------
  Kdo? Co?            subject                1st -- první    Nominative
                                             pád             

  Koho? Co?           object                 4th -- čtvrtý   Accusative
                                             pád             

  Koho? Čeho?         *of* sth/sb            2nd -- druhý    Genitive
                                             pád             

  Komu? Čemu?         *to* something         3rd -- třetí    Dative
                      /somebody              pád             

  (v/o) Kom? Čem?     in/about/\... sth/sb   6 th -- šestý   Locative
                                             pád             

  Kým? Čím?           with/by/through sth/sb 7th -- sedmý    Instrumental
                                             pád             
  ------------------- ---------------------- --------------- --------------

The traditional ordering of cases is *1. Nominative, 2. Genitive, 3.
Dative, 4. Accusative, 5.* *Vocative* (see below)*, 6. Locative, 7.
Instrumental*. But this is not based on neither logical progression nor
frequency. The order used in this book is a compromise between logical
progression and frequency with the three most frequent cases being first
and the least frequent case last.

The following sections describe the form and function of each case in
detail. They are followed by an comprehensive chart of the different
case endings.

#### XX Models

Different types of nouns and other words change their forms differently.
Each group is named after a model word and it is also called a model
(*vzor* in Czech). There are fifteen models of Czech nouns, including
the more prominent sub-models. They are all listed in the chart below.
Each model is given a common word to represent it. Different textbooks
use different example words. Here we chose ones we hope are most useful
for learners:

**TEN:** *kamarád* (friend), *muž* (man), *čaj* (tea), *rohlík* (bread
roll), *poradce* (advisor), *policista* (policeman)

**TA:** *voda* (water), *rýže* (rice), *daň* (tax), *noc* (night)

**TO:** *pivo* (beer), *kuře* (chicken), *nádraží* (station), *moře*
(sea)

All models can have further submodels for some exceptions but we ignore
these here. For example, ***rohlík*** has a common submodel ***les***
*(forest)* but we treat them as the same model. They only differ
slightly in some cases.

Model and case are often used to mean the same thing. In fact, model
describes which particular ending a word will take in a certain case.
Once you know the gender, you can most often tell the model from the
form in the *Kdo? Co?* case. But there are many words, where this is not
possible (for example, feminine (TA) nouns ending in a consonant).

##### *kamarád* and *muž* -- soft and hard animate masculines

  ---------------------------------------------------
  Singular         kamarád           muž
  ---------------- ----------------- ----------------
  **Kdo? Co?** N   kamarád-Ø         muž-Ø

  **Koho? Co?** A  kamarád**-a**     muž**-e**

  **Koho? Čeho?**  kamarád**-a**     muž**-e**
  G                                  

  **Komu? Čemu?**  kamarád**-ovi**   muž-**i**
  D                                  

  **(o) Kom?       kamarád**-ovi**   muž-**i**
  Čem?** L                           

  **Kým? Čím?** I  kamarád**-em**    muž-**em**
  ---------------------------------------------------

##### *rohlík* and *čaj* -- soft and hard inanimate masculines

##### *voda* and *rýže* -- feminines ending in a vowel

##### *noc* and *daň* - two feminines ending in a consonant

##### pivo

##### *nádraží* and *účetní* -- nouns of all genders that look like adjectives

#### Kdo? Co? - Nominative - 1. pád - 1^st^ case

###### Function

- Words in this case most often function as the subject of a sentence.

- This form is found in the dictionary

- This is the form used when naming or pointing at things (your name
  is..., this is...)

- It is the form used in comparisons using *than* (*než*)

###### Form

This is the basic form of all words. It is found in the dictionary.
Gender can be best determined based on Kdo? Co? form in instances where
it is possible. See gender.

- It is never found after a preposition.

- In a neutral sentence, it is most often found before the verb but
  often comes after the verb when you want to stress the subject.

|    | Ten (masculine)                                                       | Ta ( feminine)              | To (neuter)                    |
+:==:+:=========:+:=========:+:=========:+:=========:+:=========:+:=========:+:====:+:======:+:====:+:====:+:====:+:====:+:=======:+:======:+
|    | Animate               | Inanimate             |                       |                             |                                |
+----+-----------+-----------+-----------+-----------+-----------+-----------+------+--------+------+------+------+------+---------+--------+
| Sg | kamarád   | muž       | rohlík    | čaj       | poradce   | policista | voda | rýže   | daň  | noc  | pivo | kuře | nádraží | moře   |
+----+-----------+-----------+-----------+-----------+-----------+-----------+------+--------+------+------+------+------+---------+--------+
| Pl | -i                    | \- y      | \- e      | \- i      | \- é      | \- y | **\^** | \- ě | \- i | \- a | \-   | **\^**  | **\^** |
|    |                       |           |           |           |           |      |        |      |      |      | ata  |         |        |
+----+-----------------------+-----------+-----------+-----------+-----------+------+--------+------+------+------+------+---------+--------+

####### Exceptions in the plural

Nominative plural *TEN animate***:**

*Kamarád* nouns ending in **-an** may end in **-i** as well as **-é**.

  ---------- --------------- --------------- ----------- --------------- ---------------
  Angličan : Angličan**i** , Angličan**é**   Američan :  Američan**i** , Američan**é**

  ---------- --------------- --------------- ----------- --------------- ---------------

*Kamarád* nouns ending in -**tel** end in **-é**.

  ----------- -------------- --------- --------------
  učitel :    učitel**é** ,  ředitel : ředitel**é**

  ----------- -------------- --------- --------------

*Kamarád* one-syllable nouns end in ***-*ové** mostly in names of
nations.

  ----------------- ---------------- ---------------- ----------------
  Fin : Fin**ové,** Rus :            Švéd :           Ital :
                    Rus**ové,**      Švéd**ové,**     Ital**ové**

  ----------------- ---------------- ---------------- ----------------

Some *Kamarád* nouns change the ending accordingto the last consonant:

  ---------- ---------- --------------
  právník    k **\>** c právní**ci**

  Čech       ch **\>**  Češi
             š          

  doktor     r **\>** ř dokto**ři**

  pstruh     h **\>** z pstru**zi**
  ---------- ---------- --------------

#### Koho? Co? - Accusative - 4. pád - 4^th^ case

###### Function

- *Koho? Co?* is the most important case of the Czech language but only
  the second most frequent

- The Latin name suggests accusing. It describes the relationship of
  influence between two objects, people, or objects and people.

- Its main function is the object of the sentence.

- It is used with some prepositions (like *na*) to indicate
  directionality.

###### Form

+----+-----------------------------------------------------------------------+---------------------------------+------------------------------------+
|    | Ten (masculine)                                                       | Ta ( feminine)                  | To (neuter)                        |
+:==:+:=========:+:=========:+:=========:+:=========:+:=========:+:=========:+:====:+:======:+:======:+:======:+:======:+:======:+:=======:+:======:+
|    | Animate               | Inanimate             |                       |                                 |                                    |
+----+-----------+-----------+-----------+-----------+-----------+-----------+------+--------+--------+--------+--------+--------+---------+--------+
|    | kamarád   | muž       | rohlík    | čaj       | poradce   | policista | voda | rýže   | daň    | noc    | pivo   | kuře   | nádraží | moře   |
+----+-----------+-----------+-----------+-----------+-----------+-----------+------+--------+--------+--------+--------+--------+---------+--------+
| Sg | \- a      | \- e      | **\^**    | **\^**    | **\^**    | \- u      | \- u | \- i   | **\^** | **\^** | **\^** | **\^** | **\^**  | **\^** |
+----+-----------+-----------+-----------+-----------+-----------+-----------+------+--------+--------+--------+--------+--------+---------+--------+
| Pl | \- y      | \- e      | \- y      | \- e      | **\^**    | \- y      | \- y | **\^** | \- ě   | \- i   | -a     | -ata   | **\^**  | **\^** |
+----+-----------+-----------+-----------+-----------+-----------+-----------+------+--------+--------+--------+--------+--------+---------+--------+

(*Chleba* is an exception and doesn't follow the model of *policista*
and doesn't change with this case.)

###### Most common verbs

This case is used as the object with most verbs that take an object.
Here are the most common ones:

> **mít** (have), **chtít** (want), **znát** (know), **vidět** (see),
> **vzít** (take), **dostat** (receive), **koupit** (buy)

Reflexive verbs with **se** such as **bát se** (to be afraid, to fear)
cannot be followed by this case because **se** already is already in the
accusative. The exception is **učit se** (to study/learn) which can have
an object in the accusative in modern Czech although historically it
required a dative.

Reflexive verbs with **si** such as **brát si** (take), **umýt si**
(wash \[one's hands\]), **koupit si** (buy) can require an accusative.

###### Most common prepositions

This case is used after some prepositions for locations. The meaning is
then typically that of movement or direction. It is frequently used in
contrast with the Locative (Kom? Čem?) which signifies static location.

**na** -- *na diskoték**u*** (to the disco) vs. *na diskotéce* (at the
disco)

**před** (in front of)**, za** (behind)**, nad** (above)**, pod**
(beneath) -- always meaning in the direction

**o** -- typically used with Locative but takes Accusative in the
meaning of "bet something", i.e. *vsadit se o stovku* (bet a thousand)

**v --** typically used with Locative but takes Accusative in the
meaning of "believe in", i.e. *věřit v boha* (believe in God), Days of
the week also take Accusative after *v*.

#### Koho? Čeho? - Genitive - 2. pád - 2^nd^ case

###### Function

- The core meaning of this case is **belonging**. It is equivalent to
  the English ***of***.

- It is used with many prepositions: *z, ze* (from), *bez* (without),
  *do* ( to), *od* (from), *blízko* (close to), *vedle* ( next to),
  *během* ( during), *kromě* ( except, besides), ...

- The partitive genitive is used after units of weights and measures and
  after other words denoting quantity.

- [deset deka]{.underline} sýr**a**, [litr]{.underline} mlék**a**,
  [kilo]{.underline} pomeranč**ů**, [půl]{.underline} hodin**y**,
  [málo]{.underline} cukr**u**, [hodně]{.underline} káv**y**,
  [trochu]{.underline} mlék**a**,... [Kolik]{.underline} šunk**y**
  chcete?

- In the plural, this case is used for counting with numbers of **5 and
  above** (see section on Numerals).

- Because of its use in counting, this is the most frequent case.

###### Form

+----+-----------------------------------------------------------------------+-----------------------------+---------------------------------+
|    | Ten (masculine)                                                       | Ta ( feminine)              | To (neuter)                     |
+:==:+:=========:+:=========:+:=========:+:=========:+:=========:+:=========:+:====:+:======:+:====:+:====:+:=====:+:====:+:=======:+:======:+
|    | Animate               | Inanimate             |                       |                             |                                 |
+----+-----------+-----------+-----------+-----------+-----------+-----------+------+--------+------+------+-------+------+---------+--------+
|    | kamarád   | muž       | rohlík    | čaj       | poradce   | policista | voda | rýže   | daň  | noc  | pivo  | kuře | nádraží | moře   |
+----+-----------+-----------+-----------+-----------+-----------+-----------+------+--------+------+------+-------+------+---------+--------+
| Sg | \- a      | \- e      | -u/a      | \- e      | **\^**    | \- y      | \- y | **\^** | \- ě | \- i | \- a  | -ete | **\^**  | **\^** |
+----+-----------+-----------+-----------+-----------+-----------+-----------+------+--------+------+------+-------+------+---------+--------+
| Pl | \- ů      |           |           |           |           |           | Ø    | \- í   | \- í | \- í | **Ø** | \-   | **\^**  | \- í   |
|    |           |           |           |           |           |           |      |        |      |      |       | at   |         |        |
+----+-----------+-----------+-----------+-----------+-----------+-----------+------+--------+------+------+-------+------+---------+--------+

####### Singular

The model of *rohlík* has two alternative endings. You have to memorize
which words use which ending.

*Rohlík* nouns end in **-a** mostly in months.

> leden -- ledn**a**, únor -- únor**a**, březen -- březn**a**, duben --
> dubn**a**, květen -- květn**a**, červen -- červn**a** (The only
> regular month is listopad -- listopad**u**) and in place names ending
> in **‑ýn/‑ín** or **--ov**: Londýn -- Londýn**a**, Berlín --
> Berlín**a**, Krumlov -- Krumlov**a**

*Rohlík* and *čaj* nouns ending in **-ek, -ec, -en** drop "e" before the
ending

> hrn**e**k -- hrnk**u**, prosin**e**c -- prosinc**e**, břez**e**n -
> březn**a**

####### Plural

*Rýže* nouns ending in **-ice**, **-ile** lose the final **-e** (**Ø**)
as in

> ulic**e** -- ulic, košil**e** -- košil, Strakonic**e** -- Strakonic,
> stanic**e** - stanic

*Voda* and *pivo* nouns drop their vowel endings ( **Ø** ) and therefore
end in consonant clusters difficult to pronounce. To aid pronunciation
**-e-** is inserted.

> kamarádka -- kamarád**e**k, tužka -- tuž**e**k, barva -- bar**e**v,
> divadlo -- divad**e**l

Some nouns shorten their stem vowel.

> jm**é**no -- jm**e**n, chv**í**le -- chv**i**l, pen**í**ze - pen**ě**z

###### Most common verbs

This case is used as the object of several important verbs.

> **zeptat/ptát se *někoho*** **na něco** (ask somebody about something)
>
> **bát se *něčeho*** (to be afraid of something)

###### Most common prepositions

This case is used with many directional prepositions.

> **do** (to, into)
>
> **z** (from inside)
>
> **od** (from edge of)
>
> **u** (by, at, near), **blízko** (near)

###### Typical categories of words

This case is most likely to be used with:

- Foods when counting (pl)

- Foods when measuring

- Place names (from/to)

#### Komu? Čemu? - Dative - 3. pád - 3^rd^ case

###### Function

- **Indirect object** (John gave the book *[to]{.underline} Mary*)

- With prepositions *k* (to, toward) and *naproti* (opposite).

- The endings in singular are identical with those of *(o) Kom? Čem?*
  (Locative) *Case*.

- This is a relatively less frequent case but often needed for giving.

###### Form 

+----+-----------------------------------------------------------------------+---------------------------+-------------------------------+
|    | Ten (masculine)                                                       | Ta ( feminine)            | To (neuter)                   |
+:==:+:=========:+:=========:+:=========:+:=========:+:=========:+:=========:+:====:+:====:+:====:+:====:+:====:+:=====:+:=======:+:====:+
|    | Animate               | Inanimate             |                       |                           |                               |
+----+-----------+-----------+-----------+-----------+-----------+-----------+------+------+------+------+------+-------+---------+------+
|    | kamarád   | muž       | rohlík    | čaj       | poradce   | policista | voda | rýže | daň  | noc  | pivo | kuře  | nádraží | moře |
+----+-----------+-----------+-----------+-----------+-----------+-----------+------+------+------+------+------+-------+---------+------+
| Sg | -ovi, u   | -ovi, i   | -u        | -i        | -i        | -ovi      | -ě   | -i                 | -u   | -eti  | **\^**  | -i   |
+----+-----------+-----------+-----------+-----------+-----------+-----------+------+--------------------+------+-------+---------+------+
| Pl | -ům       |           |           |           |           |           | -ám  | -ím                | -ům  | -atům | -ím            |
+----+-----------+-----------+-----------+-----------+-----------+-----------+------+--------------------+------+-------+----------------+

When the dative is applied to two or more consecutive *TEN* nouns, the
last one uses a different ending. This is very common with names. The
last noun ends in ***-ovi*** and all preceding nouns end in **‑u*.***
For example:

> **k** Pavl**ovi**, pan**u** doktor**ovi**, pan**u** doktor**u**
> Havl**ovi**, pan**u** doktor**u** Pavl**u** Havl**ovi**

*TA* nouns change endings:

+---------+--------+-----------+:--------+
| banka   | **\>** | **k**     | k       |
|         |        | bance     | **\>**  |
|         |        |           | c       |
+---------+--------+-----------+---------+
| sprcha  | **\>** | **proti** | ch      |
|         |        | sprše     | **\>**  |
|         |        |           | š       |
+---------+--------+-----------+---------+
| opera   | **\>** | **k**     | r       |
|         |        | opeře     | **\>**  |
|         |        |           | ř       |
+---------+--------+-----------+---------+
| Praha   | **\>** | **k**     | h       |
|         |        | Praze     | **\>**  |
| Olga    |        |           | z       |
|         |        | **k**     |         |
|         |        | Olze      | g       |
|         |        |           | **\>**  |
|         |        |           | z       |
+---------+--------+-----------+---------+

###### Most common verbs

This case is used as the second or indirect object of verbs. The first
object is typically accusative.

> **dát něco *někomu*** (give something \[acc.\] to somebody\])
>
> **volat, telefonovat *někomu*** (call, phone somebody)
>
> **věřit *někomu*** (believe somebody)
>
> **ublížit *někomu*** (hurt somebody)

###### Most common prepositions

This case is used especially with one important preposition.

> **k** (toward) -- *Půjdu k Petrovi.* I will go to Peter.
>
> **proti** (against) -- *Jsem proti válce.* I'm against war.

#### (o) Kom? Čem? - Locative - 6. pád - 6^th^ case

###### Function

This case is **only used with prepositions** which indicate locality and
static nature. It never stands alone.

###### Form

+----+-----------------------------------------------------------------------+---------------------------+--------------------------------+
|    | Ten (masculine)                                                       | Ta ( feminine)            | To (neuter)                    |
+:==:+:=========:+:=========:+:=========:+:=========:+:=========:+:=========:+:====:+:====:+:====:+:====:+:====:+:======:+:=======:+:====:+
|    | Animate               | Inanimate             |                       |                           |                                |
+----+-----------+-----------+-----------+-----------+-----------+-----------+------+------+------+------+------+--------+---------+------+
|    | kamarád   | muž       | rohlík    | čaj       | poradce   | policista | voda | rýže | daň  | noc  | pivo | kuře   | nádraží | moře |
+----+-----------+-----------+-----------+-----------+-----------+-----------+------+------+------+------+------+--------+---------+------+
| Sg | -ovi\     | -ovi\     | -u\       | \- i      | \- i      | -ovi      | -ě   | -i                 | -u\  | -eti   | **\^**  | \- i |
|    | -u        | -i        | -ě        |           |           |           |      |                    | -ě   |        |         |      |
+----+-----------+-----------+-----------+-----------+-----------+-----------+------+--------------------+------+--------+---------+------+
| Pl | -ech      | \- ích                                        | -ech      | -ách | -ích               | -ech | -atech | -ích           |
+----+-----------+-----------------------------------------------+-----------+------+--------------------+------+--------+----------------+

Notice that the endings in singular are identical to those of the *Komu?
Čemu?*(Dative) case.

*TEN* nouns in the singular of *Kamarád* may have two endings in
singular: **-ovi, -u.**

**-ovi** is usually used with names, but only for the last word in a
sequence and **-u** for all the preceding words.

> **o** Pavl**ovi**, pan**u** doktor**ovi**, pan**u** doktor**u**
> Havl**ovi**, pan**u** doktor**u** Pavl**u** Havl**ovi**

###### Singular exceptions

In the singular the ending **-e** is realized in the following ways:

after **-l-, -s-,-z-** in the singular of *rohlík , voda , pivo.*

> dopis -- v dopise, obraz -- na obraze, škola -- ve škole, divadlo -- v
> divadle

after **--k-, -ch-, -r-, -h-, -g-** in the singular of *voda*

+---------+--------+---------+:--------+
| banka   | **\>** | **v**   | k       |
|         |        | bance   | **\>**  |
|         |        |         | c       |
+---------+--------+---------+---------+
| sprcha  | **\>** | **ve**  | ch      |
|         |        | sprše   | **\>**  |
|         |        |         | š       |
+---------+--------+---------+---------+
| opera   | **\>** | **v**   | r       |
|         |        | opeře   | **\>**  |
|         |        |         | ř       |
+---------+--------+---------+---------+
| Praha   | **\>** | **v**   | h       |
|         |        | Praze   | **\>**  |
| Olga    |        |         | z       |
|         |        | **o**   |         |
|         |        | Olze    | g       |
|         |        |         | **\>**  |
|         |        |         | z       |
+---------+--------+---------+---------+

(Note also papír -- na papíře)

In the singular the ending **-ě** is realized in the following ways:

after **-b-, -p-, -v-, -f-, -m-** in the singular of *rohlík, voda,
pivo*

> ryba - v rybě, káva -- v kávě, Harfa -- na Harfě, zima -- v zimě

after **-d-, -t-, -n-** in the singular of *rohlík, voda, pivo*

> oběd -- na obědě, auto -- v autě, kino -- v kině

In the singular the ending **-u** of *rohlík, pivo* is preferred
especially after **-k-, -h-, -g-, -ch-**.

> Polsko -- v Polsku, roh -- na rohu, Peking -- v Pekingu, ucho --
> v uchu

In the singular *pivo* and *rohlík* have an alternative ending for same
words which have to be memorized.

*TEN* nouns in the plural of *kamarád, rohlík, pivo* ending in **-k-,
-h-, -g-, -ch-** end in **-ích.**

  ------- -------- ---------- ---------- -------- -------- ----------- --------
  vlak    **\>**   **ve**     k **\>** c dialog   **\>**   **v**       g **\>**
                   vlacích                                 dialozích   z

  Čech    **\>**   **o**      ch **\>**  roh      **\>**   **v**       h **\>**
                   Češích     š                            rozích      z
  ------- -------- ---------- ---------- -------- -------- ----------- --------

###### Typical categories of words

This case is most likely to be used with:

- Place names (when being **in** them)

- Processes and time periods

- Personal names (when talking **about** them)

#### Kým? Čím? - Instrumental - 7. pád - 7^th^ case

###### Function

- Used as an equivalent of ***with, by, through, via*** i.e. to describe
  instruments and means (such as going by car, or digging with a
  shovel).

- Often used **with prepositions** such as ***s*** (with).

- Also used to express permanent or acquired property similarly to
  Spanish ser and estar.

- This is the least frequent of the Czech cases but it is still very
  important.

###### Form

+----+-----------------------------------------------------------------------+---------------------------+------------------------------+
|    | Ten (masculine)                                                       | Ta ( feminine)            | To (neuter)                  |
+:==:+:=========:+:=========:+:=========:+:=========:+:=========:+:=========:+:====:+:====:+:====:+:====:+:====:+:====:+:=======:+:====:+
|    | Animate               | Inanimate             |                       |                           |                              |
+----+-----------+-----------+-----------+-----------+-----------+-----------+------+------+------+------+------+------+---------+------+
|    | kamarád   | muž       | rohlík    | čaj       | poradce   | policista | voda | rýže | daň  | noc  | pivo | kuře | nádraží | moře |
+----+-----------+-----------+-----------+-----------+-----------+-----------+------+------+------+------+------+------+---------+------+
| Sg | \- em                                                     | -ou       | -ou  | -í / -ě / -i       | -em         | \- ím   | \-   |
|    |                                                           |           |      |                    |             |         | em   |
|    |                                                           |           |      |                    | -etem       |         |      |
+----+-----------+-----------+-----------+-----------+-----------+-----------+------+--------------------+------+------+---------+------+
| Pl | \- y      | \- i      | \- y      | \- i      | \- i      | \- y      | -ami | \- emi             | \- y | -aty | -ími    | \- i |
+----+-----------+-----------+-----------+-----------+-----------+-----------+------+--------------------+------+------+---------+------+

###### Most common prepositions

This case is used with several important prepositions and has the
meaning of location.

> **s** (with) -- *omeleta se sýrem* (omelette with cheese)
>
> **před** (in front of), **za** (behind)**, pod** (underneath)**, nad**
> (above, over)

###### Most common verbs

This case is mostly used with prepositions. There are only a few verbs
that take it as their second object.

> **zabít někoho *něčím*** (kill somebody \[acc.\] with something)
>
> **překvapit někoho *něčím*** (surprise somebody \[acc.\] with
> something)
>
> **rozbít něco *něčím*** (break something \[acc.\] with something)

###### Special use after *být* as acquired property

The instrumental is also used after the verb *být* (to be) especially in
the future to express becoming something. It is also used as the object
of the verb *stát se* (to become).

> budu učitel**em** (I will be a teacher)
>
> stanu se učitel**em** (I will becom a teacher)

###### Typical categories of words

This case is most likely to be used with:

- Means of transportation (travel by)

- Condiments, side dishes (food with)

- Personal names (being with)

#### Special forms for talking to people: Rules for changing people\'s names when addressing them -- another case?

*The first thing to remember:* in Czech, when you address someone you
have to change the ending of their name. It really does make a lot of
difference in establishing communication, even if you are speaking with
the person in English. Expect that Czechs will change your name, too,
when speaking to you in Czech.

*The second thing to remember:* in polite conversation with someone
older than you, or someone you don\'t know well always use their
academic title rather than their name. Thus saying something like *Mr.
Doctor*.

Traditionally, this special form is considered a case called *vocative*.
Czechs know it as the 5^th^ case (pátý pád). They all learn the rule as
*Pátým pádem oslovujeme* (we use the fifth case to address).

##### Men

+----------------+--------+--------------------+------------------------------------------+
| Ending         | \>     | Address            | Examples                                 |
+================+========+====================+==========================================+
| -b, -d, -f,    | \+     | -e                 | Roma**n \>** Roman[e]{.underline},       |
| -l,            |        |                    | Micha**l \>** Michal[e]{.underline},     |
|                |        |                    | Marti**n \>** Martin[e]{.underline},     |
| -n, -p, -r,    |        |                    | Jose**f \>** Josef[e]{.underline},       |
| -t, -v         |        |                    |                                          |
+----------------+--------+--------------------+------------------------------------------+
| \-**C**e**C**\ | **\>** | -CC[e]{.underline} | Ka**r*e*l** **\>** Karl[e]{.underline},  |
| (only the      |        |                    | Pa**v*e*l** **\>** Pavl[e]{.underline}   |
| above)         |        |                    |                                          |
+----------------+--------+--------------------+------------------------------------------+
| \-**C**r       | **\>** | \-**C**ř           | Pe**tr** **\>** Pet**ř[e]{.underline}**  |
+----------------+--------+--------------------+------------------------------------------+
| -č, -š, -ř,    | \+     | -i                 | Tomá**š** **\>** Tomáš[i]{.underline},   |
| -ž, -j, -c, -s |        |                    | Luká**š \>** Lukáš[i]{.underline},       |
+----------------+--------+--------------------+------------------------------------------+
| -k, -h, -ch,   | \+     | -u                 | Patri**k** **\>** Patrik[u]{.underline}, |
| -g             |        |                    | Domini**k** **\>**                       |
|                |        |                    | Dominik[u]{.underline}, Jindři**ch**     |
|                |        |                    | **\>** Jindřich[u]{.underline}, Gre**g   |
|                |        |                    | \>** Greg[u]{.underline}                 |
+----------------+--------+--------------------+------------------------------------------+
| -a             | **\>** | -o                 | Jirk[a]{.underline} **\>**               |
|                |        |                    | Jirk[o]{.underline}, Honz[a]{.underline} |
|                |        |                    | **\>** Honz[o]{.underline},              |
|                |        |                    | Frant[a]{.underline} **\>**              |
|                |        |                    | Frant[o]{.underline}                     |
+----------------+--------+--------------------+------------------------------------------+

##### Women

  -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
  Ending        \>       Address    Examples
  ------------- -------- ---------- -------------------------------------------
  -a            **\>**   -o         Jan**a** **\>** Jan[o]{.underline},
                                    Katk**a** **\>** Katk[o]{.underline},
                                    Veronik**a** **\>** Veronik[o]{.underline}

  -e            =        -e         Lucie **\>** Lucie, Marie **\>** Marie

  any consonant =        no change  Karin **\>** Karin, Dagmar **\>** Dagmar
  -----------------------------------------------------------------------------

##### How to address people with their full names

Same rules as for first names apply for surnames. You always prefix the
surname with

+-------------------+-------------------+
| Mr. (pan)         | Mrs (paní).       |
+===================+===================+
|   --------------- |   --------------- |
|   Novák           |   pane Nováku     |
|                   |                   |
|   Novotný         |   pane Novotný    |
|                   |                   |
|   Malá            |   paní Malá       |
|   --------------- |   --------------- |
+-------------------+-------------------+

Female surnames always end in ***á***.

##### How to address people with academic titles

This is very important. You only use the polite Mr. or Mrs. with the
title without the name.

  -------------------------------------------------------------------
  Men:                             Women:           
  --------------- ---------------- ---------------- -----------------
  Mgr. Malý       pane magistře    Mgr. Reslová     paní magistro

  dr. Novák       pane doktore     PhDr. Nováková   paní doktorko

  ing. Pinc       pane inženýre    ing. Pincová     paní inženýrko

  doc. Šebák      pane docente     doc. Šebáková    paní docentko

  prof. Machovec  pane profesore   prof. Machovcová paní profesorko
  -------------------------------------------------------------------

Note that the female titles append *ka* to them, which changes to *ko*
when addressing them.

##### Informal, Endearing ways to address someone

Czechs sometimes use a special form of the first name to address people.
This consists of the shortening of the name (unless the name is already
short) and adding the ending **-i** for male or female names or
occasionally **-uš** or **-u** for some female names. This is very
informal and used only with the closest of friends in the context of
endearment. Also, it does not work with surnames. In loving contexts,
the **-i** is often lengthened to **-í.**

  -------------------------------------------------------------------
  Male names:                      Female names:    
  --------------- ---------------- ---------------- -----------------
  Martin          Marti            Jana             Jani

  Petr            Petři            Petra            Petři, Petru,
                                                    Petruš

  Karel           Karli            Veronika         Veru

  Honza           Honzi            Jarmila          Jaru, Jaruš

  Standa          Standi                            
  -------------------------------------------------------------------

**Note:** Not all names lend themselves to this form as well as others.
Long male names ending in hard consonants such as Michal, Patrik or
female names ending in consonants such as Karin. However, you will find
that families may find other nicknames for people with these names to
which this form can be added. Often, this form is only suitable to use
within a family or a close group of friends.

###### Shortening female names

  ruka      ruce                              hands

  oko       oči                               eyes

  ucho      uši                               ears

  člověk    lidi                              man/person -
                                              people

  šortky                                         shorts

  kalhoty                                        pants

  hodinky                                        watch

  potraviny                                      grocery store

  peníze                                         money

  České Budějovice, Malešice, Hodkovice          town name

  ------------------------------------------ --- -------------

##### How to say \"a pair of shorts, two pairs of ... , ten pairs of ...\"

All of the above plural words can be counted using a special form of
numbers:

  -------------- ---------------------------------------- ---------------
  jedn**y**      šortky, kalhoty, hodinky, noviny, \....  one pair of
                                                          \...

  dvoje          šortky, kalhoty, hodinky, noviny, \....  two pairs of
                                                          \...

  troje          šortky, kalhoty, hodinky, noviny, \....  three pairs of
                                                          \...

  čtv**ery**     šortky, kalhoty, hodinky, noviny, \....  four pairs of
                                                          \...

  pat**ery**     šortky, kalhoty, hodinky, noviny, \....  five pairs of
                                                          \...

  šest**ery**    šortky, kalhoty, hodinky, noviny, \....  six pairs of
                                                          \...

  sedm**ery**    šortky, kalhoty, hodinky, noviny, \....  seven pairs of
                                                          \...

  ...                                                     

  desa**tery**   šortky, kalhoty, hodinky, noviny, \....  ten pairs of
                                                          \...
  -------------- ---------------------------------------- ---------------

#### Softening -- Palatalization

Some endings in Czech nouns soften the preceding consonant. This is
sometimes called palatalization because the roof of the tongue moves to
or towards the palate to produce the new consonant. The endings are:
*Kdo? Co? TEN animate nouns plural* (kamarád)*,* and *Komu? Čemu?/Kom?
Čem? TA nouns in singular* (voda)

##### TEN animate nouns - kamarád, doktor, odborník \...

  --------- ----------------------- -----------
  k **\>**  odborník **\>**         (expert)
  c         odborníci               

  ch **\>** Čech **\>** Češi        (Czech)
  š                                 

  r **\>**  doktor **\>** doktoři   (doctor)
  ř                                 

  h **\>**  pstruh **\>** pstruzi   (trout)
  z                                 
  --------- ----------------------- -----------

##### TA nouns - Praha, voda, taška

  --------- --------------------- ----------
  k **\>**  banka **\>** bance    (bank)
  c                               

  ch **\>** *sprcha **\>** sprše* (shower)
  š                               

  r **\>**  opera **\>** opeře    (opera)
  ř                               

  h **\>**  *Praha**\>** Praze*   (Prague)
  z                               
  --------- --------------------- ----------

  ------- --------------------------------------------------------------------
  **!**   Endings that cause palatalization are typically marked with an
          asterisk (\*) in the charts in this book.

  ------- --------------------------------------------------------------------

##### Palatalization of Adjectives

See section on adjectives.

#### All models of Czech nouns - Case Endings

| 12. Singular | Ten                                                         | Ta                                     | To                                        |

| **Kdo? Co?** | kamarád | muž     | čaj     | rohlík  | poradce | policista | voda    | rýže   | daň      | noc      | pivo     | kuře     | nádraží  | moře     |
| N            |         |         |         |         |         |           |         |        |          |          |          |          |          |          |
+--------------+---------+---------+---------+---------+---------+-----------+---------+--------+----------+----------+----------+----------+----------+----------+
| **Koho?      | -a      | -e      | **\^**                      | -u                  | -i     | **\^**                                                          |
| Co?** A      |         |         |                             |                     |        |                                                                 |
+--------------+---------+---------+---------+---------+---------+---------------------+--------+----------+----------+----------+----------+---------------------+
| **Koho?      | -a      | -e                | -u /-a  | **\^**  | -y                  | **\^** | -ě       | -i       | -a       | -ete     | **\^**              |
| Čeho?** G    |         |                   |         |         |                     |        |          |          |          |          |                     |

Asterisk (\*) signifies endings that change the form of the last
consonant in the word. See section ***Palatalizacion -- Softening***
above.

|          |          |            |            |         |             | alb**y**   |            |
+----------+----------+------------+------------+---------+-------------+------------+------------+

Some of these models are fairly common (such as *muzeum/album*) while
others only apply to a few nouns. *Les* is the only lesser model used
almost exclusively for nouns of Czech origin.

\* This model is only used for male names ending in --í of which Jiří is
the only Czech example. However, it is also used for all foreign male
names ending in --y such as Henry, Woody.

\*\* This model is only used for female names of foreign origin ending
in a consonant or --y. All forms are the same.

**†** Nouns belonging to this model contain many exceptions. Some can
take the endings on regular masculine inanimates ending in a hard
consonant (e.g. status, status, statusu, statusu, statusu, statusem) and
sometimes the declension is mixed. This varies much from speaker to
speaker. *Autobus* doesn't belong to the model. If the noun ending in
*--us* is a name such as *Spartakus*, *Ikarus*, etc., then regular
*kamarád* endings replace the *--us*.

**‡** *Album* only differs from *museum* in the plural. The difference
is whether the *--um* is preceded by a vowel or a consonant.

#### Most useful noun endings

Learning all the endings of Czech nouns may seem daunting. However, most
of them are only used quite rarely. You will need to learn them
eventually but, in the meantime, you can get very far with just six
including when no ending is added, which is also important. They are:
-y/i, -a, -u, - Ø, -ů, -e/-ě and are listed roughly in order of
frequency and importance.

As you can see, each of these endings can have multiple function and can
be used different with different types of nouns. But if you ever need to
guess how to change a noun, you will probably get much closer to the
correct one with the top 3 on this list than any other. You will often
sound better with the wrong ending than with no ending at all.

+-----------+-------------+--------------+--------------+-------------+-----------------+
| Case      | Kdo? Co? /  | Koho? Co?    | Koho? Čeho?  | Gentive pl  | v Kom? Čem?     |
|           | Koho? Co?   |              |              |             |                 |
|           |             | Accusative   | Genitive Sg  |             | Komu? Čemu?     |
|           | Nom / acc   |              |              |             |                 |
|           | plural      |              |              |             | Locative/dative |
+:==========+:============+:=============+:=============+:============+:================+
| **Uses**  | counting    | Object, *na* | prepositions | counting    | prepositions    |
|           | 2-4         |              | *z/do\*      | over 5      | *v* / *k*       |
|           |             |              | volume       |             |                 |
|           | Object      |              |              |             |                 |

|           |             |              | z/do         |             |                 |

|           |             |              |              |             |                 |
|           |             |              | auto         |             |                 |
|           |             |              | kamarád**a** |             |                 |
+-----------+-------------+--------------+--------------+-------------+-----------------+

|           |             | káv**u**     | cukr**u**    |             |                 |

### Adjectives

There are two types of adjectives in Czech. One type ends in a *hard
consonant* **+ ý, á, é**; and the other in a *soft consonant* **+ í** in
the Kdo? Co? case.

#### Hard Adjectives

Adjectives where the last consonant is: ***-h-, -ch-, -k-, -r-, -d-,
-t-, -n-*** (see *Pronunciation* for more details).

+-----------+--------------------------+-----------------------+--------------------+---------------------+
| Singular  | ten (živý)               | ten (neživý)          | ta                 | to                  |
+:==========+=============+============+=============+=========+============+=======+=============+=======+
| **Kdo?    | dobr**ý**   | kamarád    | dobr**ý**   | banán   | dobr**á**  | voda  | dobr**é**   | pivo  |
| Co?** N   |             |            |             |         |            |       |             |       |
+-----------+-------------+------------+-------------+---------+------------+-------+-------------+-------+
| **Koho?   | dobrého     | kamaráda   | dobr**ý**   | banán   | dobr**ou** | vodu  | dobr**é**   | pivo  |
| Co?** A   |             |            |             |         |            |       |             |       |
+-----------+-------------+------------+-------------+---------+------------+-------+-------------+-------+
| **Koho?   | dobr**ého** | kamaráda   | dobr**ého** | banánu  | dobr**é**  | vody  | dobr**ého** | piva  |
| Čeho?** G |             |            |             |         |            |       |             |       |
+-----------+-------------+------------+-------------+---------+------------+-------+-------------+-------+
| **Komu?   | dobr**ému** | kamarádovi | dobr**ému** | banánu  | dobr**é**  | vodě  | dobr**ému** | pivu  |
| Čemu?** D |             |            |             |         |            |       |             |       |

| Kom?      |             |            |             |         |            |       |             |       |
| Čem?** L  |             |            |             |         |            |       |             |       |
+-----------+-------------+------------+-------------+---------+------------+-------+-------------+-------+
| **Kým?    | dobr**ým**  | kamarádem  | dobr**ým**  | banánem | dobr**ou** | vodou | dobrým      | pivem |
| Čím?** I  |             |            |             |         |            |       |             |       |
+-----------+-------------+------------+-------------+---------+------------+-------+-------------+-------+

#### Soft Adjectives

-j-,-ď-, -ť-, -ň-** (see *Pronunciation* for more details).

pronounced *ď, ť, ň*.

| Co?** N   |             |            |             |         |           |       |             |       |
