#### Modal verbs: can, must, need, should

Unlike English, Czech doesn't have modal verbs as such that are set
apart from other verbs by many formal features. It may be useful
however, to list the Czech equivalent expressions to English modal
verbs. They are often followed by the infinitive.

**Note** that negating certain modal verbs in Czech produces an
unexpected translation in English.

  ---------------- ------------------------------------------------------
  must, have to    **muset** (musím, musíš, musí, ..., musí)

  must not, may    **nesmět** (nesmím, nesmíš, nesmí, nesmíme, nesmíte,
  not              nesmí)

  not have to      **nemuset** (nemusím, ...)

  can              **moct** (můžu, můžeš, může, ..., můžou)

  may              **smět** (smím, smíš, smí, smíme, smíte, smí)

  need             **potřebovat** (-uju,-uješ,-uje,-ujeme,...,-ujou)

  should/ ought to měl(a, i) bych, bys, by, bysme, byste, by + infinitive

  should           mám, máš, ..., mají + infinitive
  ---------------- ------------------------------------------------------

##### Must not and cannot (Negating modal verbs)

+:-------------------+:-------------------+:----------------+:----------------+
| ANO                                     | NE                                |
+--------------------+--------------------+-----------------+-----------------+
| muset              | must               | nesmím          | I must not      |
+--------------------+--------------------+-----------------+-----------------+
| muset              | have to            | nemusím         | I don\'t have   |
|                    |                    |                 | to              |
+--------------------+--------------------+-----------------+-----------------+
| smět               | may                | nesmím          | I may not       |
+--------------------+--------------------+-----------------+-----------------+
| moct               | can                | nemůžu          | I cannot        |
+--------------------+--------------------+-----------------+-----------------+
| potřebovat         | I need             | nepotřebuju     | I don\'t need   |
+--------------------+--------------------+-----------------+-----------------+

#### Reflexive or self (*se* and *si*) verbs

Some verbs only (or mostly) appear with one of the reflexive pronouns
(*se* or *si*). Reflexives express a direction to the speaker. As in *I
saw myself.* or *She gave this to herself.* In Czech, it is used even
when the 'reflexive' meaning is hidden and becomes a part of the verb.

The *se* or *si* particles take up the Koho? Co? or Komu? Čemu? case
slots which means that the following noun has to take a different form.

##### *se* verbs

*se* reflexive verbs are never followed by a noun in the Koho? Co? form
because *se* already fills that role. We can divide them by what
follows:

###### True reflexive (my self)

You can add *se* to most verbs to express the idea of *self*.

**uhodit se** to hit onself

**topit se / utopit se** to drown

###### Non-reflexive *se* verbs

These verbs no longer have a very clear reflexive meaning but are always
used with *se*.

###### *se* verbs with *Kdo? Co?*

**jmenovat se** to be called/named

###### *se* verbs with *Koho? Čeho?*

**bát se** to be afraid of something

**ptát se / zeptat se** to ask \[of\] someone

###### *se* verbs with *Komu? Čemu?*

**líbit se** to be liked (lit. to be pleasurable to someone)

**nabídnout se** to offer self to someone

###### *se* verbs with *Kým? Čím?*

**bavit se** to have a good time; lit. to be amused by something

**zabývat se** to research something

###### *se* verbs with a preposition

**dívat se na / podívat se na** to look at something

**líbat se s** to kiss someone; lit. to kiss with someone . This is
similar to make out with someone. **Note**: this is different from to
give a kiss to someone which is *políbit někoho*.

###### Mutual *se*

**potkat se** to meet (each other)

**honit se** to rush, to chase (each other)

###### *se* passive

**Říká se.** They say (it is said)

**Rozbilo se to.** It broke (it was broken)

##### *si* verbs

*si* reflexive verbs are never followed by a noun in the Komu? Čemu?
form because *si* already fills that role. We can divide them into
several types:

###### True *si* reflexive

These really express a direction to oneself.

**dát si** to have (lit. to give to oneself)

###### Non-reflexive *si* verbs

These verbs are always used with *si* but don't have a reflexive meaning
anymore.

**číst si** to read (lit. to read to oneself)

###### Mutual *si* (to each other)

*Si* is also used to express mutual meaning when two people or things
give something to each other.

psát si to write to each other

###### Casual (narrative) *si*

This is a very interesting use of *si* (a part of what is sometimes
called ethical dative). You can add *si* to almost any verb to express
the meaning of casualness *by your leave*, *as if nothing happened*.

My utíkáme a on si jde. We're running and he's walking (as if nothing
happened).

#### Verbs of Motion -- Their Aspect and Tense

Verbs of motion form a separate category in Czech with respect to both
tense and aspect. Although there are only roughly 15 of them, they are
very important in Czech because, through the use of different prefixes,
**they appear at the core of more than 500 possible dictionary
entries**. They differ from the other verbs in two respects. 1) **Future
tense is formed with a prefix *po-*,** and 2) they have **a special form
for the repetitive aspect** (where other verbs use the imperfective
form).

Also, some of them make a **distinction between movement on foot and by
a vehicle** (as in *jít* vs. *jet* or *nést* vs. *vézt*).

**Note:** Some verbs only display some characteristics of verbs of
motion.

The verbs most commonly listed as verbs of motion are:

  ------------------ ------------ ---------------- -------------------------------
  **Imperfective**   **Future**   **Repetitive**   **Translation**

  jít                půjdu        chodit           to go (on foot)

  jet                pojedu       jezdit           to go (by vehicle)

  běžet              poběžím      běhat            to run

  letět              poletím      létat            to fly

  vést               povedu       vodit            to lead

  nést               ponesu       nosit            to carry (in arms/on foot)

  vézt               povezu       vozit            to carry (in a vehicle)

  hnát               poženu       honit            to chase

  táhnout            potáhnu      tahat            to pull, drag

  lézt               polezu       lézt             to crawl
  ------------------ ------------ ---------------- -------------------------------

Verbs of motion are further special in that they can each take on **the
full range of the 20 prefixes** in Czech. Sometimes the prefix will only
add its meaning to the verb, for example to indicate direction, as in
*obejít* (to walk around, circumvent) but sometimes it will create a
whole new word where the original meaning of the verb is barely
recognizable, as in *najít* (to find).

By attaching a prefix to an imperfective verb of motion, the verb
becomes perfective. It could then be said that **each imperfective verb
of motion has 20 perfective equivalents** (see above for hubs of verbs)
with slightly different meaning. The confusion steps in when we realize
that **each of these newly created perfective verbs has in turn an
imperfective pair** without any alteration of meaning. This pair verb is
created using the repetitive verb of motion as its root. That is shown
in a table below using the prefix *při-* (in the direction toward the
point of reference). The same holds for all the remaining 19 prefixes.

  ------------------ -------------- ---------------- --------------------------------
  **Imperfective**   **Perfective   **Imperfective   **Translation**
                     with při-**    with při-**      

  jít                přijít         přicházet        to come (on foot)

  jet                přijet         přijíždět        to arrive (by vehicle)

  běžet              přiběhnout     přibíhat         to run (toward)

  letět              přiletět       přilétat         to fly (toward)

  vést               přivést        přivádět         to lead (toward)

  nést               přinést        přinášet         to carry (in arms/on foot --
                                                     toward)

  vézt               přivézt        přivážet         to carry (in a vehicle toward)

  hnát               přihnat        přihánět         to chase (toward)

  táhnout            přitáhnout     přitahovat       to pull (toward)

  lézt               přilézt        přilézat         to crawl (toward)
  ------------------ -------------- ---------------- --------------------------------

#### Models of Verbs for Conjugation -- Complete Chart

The traditional full classification of Czech verbal conjugation uses the
following five classes with subcategories, giving a total of 14 models
distinguished by the ending in the third person. Even that is not enough
for a full classification but it covers the majority of Czech verbs.
Fronek's Czech-English/English-Czech Dictionary gives the complete
breakdown. There are other ways to organize the verbs as well.

  ----------- ------------- --------------- -- ------------------ -------------------------------------
  **Model**   **Present**   **Past**           **INFINITIVE**     (C = consonant; V = Vowel)

  **-e**      -u/-i, nese   nesl               **-Ct**            vézt, lézt, vést, číst, krást, třást,
                                                                  kvést, mást

              -u/-i, peče   pekl               **-ct/-ci**        moct, téct, vléct

              -u/-i, bere   bral               **-át**            prát, žrát, hnát, koupat, kopat,
                                                                  chápat, plavat, kašlat, lámat, klamat

              -u/-i, maže   mazal              **-at**            lhát, psát, poslat, vázat, kousat,
                                                                  skákat, páchat, řezat

              -u/-i, tře    třel               **-ít**            umřít, zavřít, přít, dřít, vřít

  **-ne**     -nu, tiskne   tiskl/tisknul      **-C+nout**        risknout, písknout, zapnout, vypnout,
                                                                  obléknout, stárnout, škrábnout,
                                                                  nadchnout, zamknout, odemknout

              -nu, mine     minul              **-V+nout**        zapomenout, vzpomenout, linout,
                                                                  vinout, stát se

              -nu/-mu,      začal/začnul       **-nout/-ít**      zatnout, vyjmout, přijmout, pojmout,
              začne                                               vzít

  **-je**     -ju, kryje    kryl               **-ýt/-ít/-out/\   pít, zabít, žít, mýt, obout, zout,
                                               -ět/-át**          hřát, hrát, dít se, smát se

              -ju, kupuje   kupoval            **-ovat**          pracovat, studovat, telefonovat,
                                                                  kontrolovat, opakovat, bojovat,
                                                                  cestovat, sportovat, stopovat

  **-í**      -ím, prosí    prosil             **-it/-ít**        učit, končit, mluvit, půjčit, pustit,
                                                                  čistit, ublížit,

              -ím, trpí,    trpěl              **-ět/et (-ít)**   umět, večeřet, letět, sedět, vidět,
              ..., -í                                             rozumět, vědět, ležet, myslet

              -ím, sází,    sázel              **-et (-ít)**      muset, bydlet, smět, znít
              \..., -ejí                                          

  -á          -ám, dělá     dělal              -at/-át            znát, koukat, dát, začínat, obědvat,
                                                                  snídat, ptát se, znát, nechat
  ----------- ------------- --------------- -- ------------------ -------------------------------------

These are some common verbs that are **irregular** in one or more
aspects: spát, mít, být, chtít, vědět, říct, jíst, moct, bát se, stát,
jít, vést, číst, krást, třást, hnát, lhát, psát, poslat, vázat, kousat,
skákat, páchat, řezat, stát se, zatnout, vzít, vyjmout, přijmout,
pojmout, obout, zout, hřát, hrát, dít se, smát se, myslet (see details
below for selected verbs).

**Note 1:** Verbs in classes I-IV are further subdivided according to
the past, the 3^rd^ person plural forms, or palatalization of the last
consonant.

**Note 2:** The numbers of these categories have no correspondence to
numbers of categories in the conjugation summary.

#### Selected (most common) Irregular Verbs

Some of these 21 verbs are not strictly speaking irregular but from the
point of view of the student it is best to treat them this way. Verbs
can also be irregular in some of their other forms such as passive.
These are not included in this chart. There are many more irregular
verbs but they are rare. *Být* is not included in this list.

#### Top 150 Czech Verbs

##### Verb chart

The verbs in this chart are the most common or most commonly needed in
Czech. They are not most frequent because frequency is dependent on the
type of text. Written, spoken, fiction, non-fiction, journalism, all of
these have different frequencies of verbs. These are judged to be most
useful for the student. They are sorted alphabetically and not by
frequency.

##### How to use the verb chart

###### Verb

The verb as it is found in the dictionary. Typically only one aspect is
given for each pair. It is the one that seems to be more common
(whenever possible).

**Note on *se*** **and *si*:** If *se* or *si* can be used with the
verb, they are listed in parentheses. If they are not in parentheses,
the verb can only be used with them. If used optionally, they typically
change the meaning (at least to *self*) this is then given under English
Translation also in parentheses.

###### Present

The conjugation is given in the forms of: first person singular (I),
third person singular (he, she, it) and third person plural (they). When
a verb has an irregular conjugation, these are the forms likely to be
different.

###### L-form (past)

This is the form of the verb used in the past tense. **--l** means that
the final **--t** of the infinitive is simple replaced with the **--l**.
Otherwise the full **--l** form is given. Some verbs have more than one
possible form. The more formal (written) is given first.

###### N-form (passive)

Although the --**n** form is mostly regular, the full form is given for
all verbs. Not all verbs can be used in a passive sentence but most need
this form to make nouns and adjectives.

###### Imperative

Even though, the imperative is almost always regular, the full singular
form is given for all verbs except for verbs ending in **--ovat** which
only replace the infinitive ending with **--uj**. The plural form can
always be formed according to the rules listed under Imperative.

###### Object

Sometimes called valence, this indicates what type of object is the verb
likely to have. The question words indicate whether it is a noun, a noun
following a preposition, or an adverbial phrase of time or place. The
question word for the noun also indicates the case.

When two objects are separated by comma, it means, that one or the other
can be chosen. When they are not, it means that both have to be present
at once (although the exact word order may be different).

Objects listed in parentheses are optional. Those given without any
marks, always need to be present in some form.

The slot marked by *koho* (for accusative) can be filled by *se* and
*komu* (for dative) can be filled by *si* if one or both are marked as
possible in the first column.

###### Aspect 

**Ipf.** marks an imperfective verb and **Pf.** marks a perfective verb.

###### Pair (same meaning)

If the verb has a pair verb, with the same meaning but of the opposite
aspect, it is listed here. **Note:** It may not be conjugated according
to the same pattern. See Irregular verbs below to check whether it
requires special treatment.

###### Prefix Derivates (diff meaning)

If there are verbs derived from this one by a prefix, the most important
ones are listed here. They always have a slightly different meaning than
the verb in the first column (which is not marked, however).

###### English Translation

The most straightforward English translation possible is given here. See
a larger dictionary for more.

### Adverbs

#### Manner

These answer the question *Jak?* (How)

  ---------- -------- --------- --------- ------
  **Jak?**   dobře    rychle    hezky     \...

             špatně   pomalu    ošklivě   \...
  ---------- -------- --------- --------- ------

+----------------+-----------+------------------------+
| jaký?          |           | jak?                   |
+----------------+-----------+------------------------+
| dobrý          | ý \> e/ě  | dob[ře]{.underline}    |
+----------------+           +------------------------+
| špatný         |           | špatn[ě]{.underline}   |
+----------------+           +------------------------+
| krásný         |           | krásn[ě]{.underline}   |
+----------------+           +------------------------+
| mladý          |           | mlad[ě]{.underline}    |
+----------------+           +------------------------+
| starý          |           | stař[e]{.underline}    |
+----------------+           +------------------------+
| drahý          |           | draz[e]{.underline}    |
+----------------+           +------------------------+
| dlouhý         |           | dlouz[e]{.underline}   |
+----------------+-----------+------------------------+
| hezký          | zký \>    | hezk[y]{.underline}    |
|                | zky       |                        |
+----------------+-----------+------------------------+
| český          | ský \>    | česk[y]{.underline}    |
|                | sky       |                        |
+----------------+-----------+------------------------+
| anglický       | cký \>    | anglick[y]{.underline} |
|                | cky       |                        |
+----------------+-----------+------------------------+
| jasný          | ý \> o    | jasn[o]{.underline}    |
+----------------+-----------+------------------------+
| dlouhý         | ý \> o    | dlouh[o]{.underline}   |
+----------------+-----------+------------------------+

+-------+------------------------------------------------------------------+
| **!** | Most of adverbs in Czech are formed from adjectives.             |
|       |                                                                  |
|       | - The ***-ý*** or ***-í*** ending of the adjective is dropped,   |
|       |   and ***-e*** or ***-ě*** is added. Remember that **-*ě***      |
|       |   changes the consonant before it.                               |
|       |                                                                  |
|       | - dobrý - dobře, jarní - jarně, mladý - mladě                    |
|       |                                                                  |
|       | k **\>** c krátký **\>** krátce                                  |
|       |                                                                  |
|       | ch **\>** š jednoduchý **\>** jednoduše                          |
|       |                                                                  |
|       | r **\>** ř starý **\>** staře                                    |
|       |                                                                  |
|       | h **\>** z drahý **\>** draze                                    |
|       |                                                                  |
|       | - The ***-cký, -ský, zký*** endings are shortened                |
|       |                                                                  |
|       | <!-- -->                                                         |
|       |                                                                  |
|       | - hezký - hezky, český - česky                                   |
|       |                                                                  |
|       | <!-- -->                                                         |
|       |                                                                  |
|       | - The ***-ý*** ending has an equivalent ***-o*** which is used   |
|       |   for some words. Sometimes, there is a slight difference in     |
|       |   meaning, which has to be learned with individual words in      |
|       |   certain contexts.                                              |
|       |                                                                  |
|       | <!-- -->                                                         |
|       |                                                                  |
|       | - daleký - daleko; dlouhý - dlouho; jasný -- jasno               |
+-------+------------------------------------------------------------------+

#### Grade of Quantity

These answer the question *Kolik?* (How much/many?)

  --------- -------- -------- ------- ------
  Kolik?    hodně    velmi    moc     \...

            málo     dost             \...
  --------- -------- -------- ------- ------

#### Comparing Adverbs - More and Most

+-----------+--------------+----------------+
| much      | more         | the most       |
+-----------+--------------+----------------+
| hodně     | více         | **nej**více    |
|           |              |                |
| moc       |              |                |
+-----------+--------------+----------------+
| málo      | méně         | **nej**méně    |
+-----------+--------------+----------------+
| dobře     | lépe         | nejlépe        |
+-----------+--------------+----------------+
| špatně    | hůř          | nejhůř         |
+-----------+--------------+----------------+
| brzo/y    | dřív(e)      | **nej**dřív(e) |
+-----------+--------------+----------------+
| rychle    | rychl**eji** | nejrychleji    |
+-----------+--------------+----------------+
| levn**ě** | levn**ěji**  | nejlevněji     |
+-----------+--------------+----------------+

+---------+----------------+----------------+----------------+----------------+
| více                     | než \...       |                | more than \... |
+--------------------------+----------------+----------------+----------------+
| nejvíce                  | z koho, čeho   |                | the most of    |
|                          | \...           |                | \...           |
+---------+----------------+----------------+----------------+----------------+
| !       | ADJECTIVE + **noun \>** *Tomáš je* **lepší** student *než já.*    |
|         | (Tomáš is a better student than I)                                |
|         |                                                                   |
|         | ADVERB + **verb \>** Učí se **lépe** *než já.* (He learns better  |
|         | than I do.)                                                       |
+---------+-------------------------------------------------------------------+

#### Time

These are used to answer the question *Kdy?* (When), *Odkdy?*(Since
when?), *Dokdy?* (Till when):

  ------------ ------------ ---------- ------------ ------------
  včera        předtím      teď/nyní   potom        zítra

  yesterday    before       now        after        tomorrow
  ------------ ------------ ---------- ------------ ------------

***stále*** (always), ***občas*** (sometimes), ***tehdy*** (then),
***dosud*** (until now)

### Prepositions

#### Time

+:----------+:--------+:--------------------+:--+:--------+:---------------+
| **v/ve**  | Koho?   | v 5 hodin, v úterý  |   | at      | 5 o\'clock     |
|           | Co?     |                     |   |         |                |
|           |         |                     |   | on      | Tuesday        |
+-----------+---------+---------------------+---+---------+----------------+
| **v/ve**  | Kom?    | v lednu, v zimě, v  |   | in      | January,       |
|           | Čem?    | roce 1948           |   |         | winter, 1948   |
+-----------+---------+---------------------+---+---------+----------------+
| **během** | Koho?   | během léta          |   | during  | during summer  |
|           | Čeho?   |                     |   |         |                |
|           |         | během hodiny        |   | in      | in an hour     |
+-----------+---------+---------------------+---+---------+----------------+
| **kolem** | Koho?   | kolem páté          |   | around  | five o\' clock |
|           | Čeho?   |                     |   |         |                |
+-----------+---------+---------------------+---+---------+----------------+
| **přes**  | Koho?   | přes den, přes      |   | during  | during the     |
|           | Co?     | víkend              |   |         | day, during    |
|           |         |                     |   |         | the weekend    |
+-----------+---------+---------------------+---+---------+----------------+
| **za**    | Koho?   | za jeden den        |   | in,     | in/after one   |
|           | Co?     |                     |   | after   | day            |
+-----------+---------+---------------------+---+---------+----------------+
| **po**    | Kom?    | po obědě, po        |   | after   | after lunch,   |
|           | Čem?    | prázdninách         |   |         | after vacation |
+-----------+---------+---------------------+---+---------+----------------+
| **od ...  | Koho?   | od pondělí do pátku |   | from    | from Monday    |
| do**      | Čeho?   |                     |   | ...     | till Sunday,   |
|           |         | od osmi do devíti   |   | till    | from 8 to 9    |
|           |         |                     |   | ...     |                |
+-----------+---------+---------------------+---+---------+----------------+
| **před**  | Kým?    | před rokem, před    |   | before, | a year, month  |
|           | Čím?    | hodinou             |   | ago     | ago            |
+-----------+---------+---------------------+---+---------+----------------+
| **k**     | Komu?   | k večeru, k ránu    |   | toward  | toward the     |
|           | Čemu?   |                     |   |         | evening        |
+-----------+---------+---------------------+---+---------+----------------+
| **na**    | Koho?   | na týden, na        |   | for     | for a week,    |
|           | Co?     | prázdniny           |   |         | for a holiday  |
+-----------+---------+---------------------+---+---------+----------------+
| **o**     | Kom?    | o víkendu, o        |   | on      | on the weekend |
|           | Čem?    | prázdninách         |   |         |                |
|           |         |                     |   | during  | during         |
|           |         |                     |   |         | vacation       |
+-----------+---------+---------------------+---+---------+----------------+
| **ob**    | Koho?   | ob týden, ob den    |   | every   | every other    |
|           | Co?     |                     |   | other   | day, week      |
+-----------+---------+---------------------+---+---------+----------------+

#### Space - *Kde?* nebo *Kam?*

Czech distinguishes between locality and directionality rather
religiously. All prepositions of space observe this distinction. It is
important whether the object is standing still or moving. For instance:
*auto jede na hory* (a car is going to the mountains) vs. *auto jede na
horách* ( car is in the mountains). What tell these two sentences apart
is the case ending.

There are two prepositions for each meaning ( e.g. *in* vs. *into*). One
set uses the case to distinguish while the other set uses two different
prepositions.

+:------------+:-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------+:------------+:--+:------------+:------------------------------------------------------------------------------------+:-------------+

|                                                                                                                |   |                                                                                                                  |
| **stationary**                                                                                                 |   | **directionally**                                                                                                |

+-------------+------------------------------------------------------------------------------------+-------------+---+-------------+-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------+--------------+

|             | height="0.38680555555555557in"}                                                    |             |   |             | height="0.49191054243219595in"}                                                     |              |
| \(o\) Kom?  |                                                                                    |             |   | Koho? Co?   |                                                                                     |              |
| Čem?        |                                                                                    |             |   |             |                                                                                     |              |
+-------------+------------------------------------------------------------------------------------+-------------+---+-------------+-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------+--------------+

                 Čeho?**

                 Čeho?**

                 Čeho?**

#### Other Uses of *o, v, za, z*

  -------------- -------------- --------------------
  o              **Koho? Co?**  sth. (as in bet
                                sth., be afraid for
                                sth)

  v              **Koho? Co?**  in (as in believe
                                in.)

  za             **Koho?        during the time of
                 Čeho?**        

  z              **Koho?        down from
                 Čeho?**

### Conjunctions

#### List of Most Common Conjunctions

##### Conjunctives

+:---------+:-----------------------------+:--------------------------+
| **a**    | Učím se česky a francouzsky. | I am studying Czech and   |
|          |                              | French.                   |
| and      | Snídám housku a čaj.         |                           |
|          |                              | I have roll and tea for   |
|          | Půjdu domů a uvařím večeři.  | breakfast.                |
|          |                              |                           |
|          |                              | I\'ll go home and cook    |
|          |                              | dinner.                   |
+----------+------------------------------+---------------------------+
| **i**    | Mám hlad i žízeň.            | I\'m hungry as well as    |
|          |                              | thirsty.                  |
| as well  | Zajímá mě literatura i       |                           |
| as       | historie.                    | I\'m interested in        |
|          |                              | literature and history.   |
| both...  | Na zahradě máme ovoce i      |                           |
| and      | zeleninu.                    | We have fruits as well as |
|          |                              | vegetables\...            |
+----------+------------------------------+---------------------------+
| **jako\  | Bylo to, jakoby se mu to     | It was as though in a     |
| jakoby** | zdálo.                       | dream.                    |
|          |                              |                           |
| as\      | Cítil se jako doma.          | He felt like he was at    |
| as       |                              | home.                     |
| though   |                              |                           |
+----------+------------------------------+---------------------------+

##### Disjunctives

+:---------+:----------------------------+:----------------------------+
| **ale**  | Chci jít do divadla, ale    | I want to go to the theater |
|          | musím jet do Prahy.         | but I must go to Prague.    |
| but      |                             |                             |
|          | Ten dům je hezký, ale       | The house is nice but       |
|          | drahý.                      | expensive.                  |
+----------+-----------------------------+-----------------------------+
| **buď -  | Buď se budeš učit německy   | You will either learn       |
| nebo**   | nebo anglicky.              | German or English.          |
|          |                             |                             |
| either   | Dal jsem to buď Pavle nebo  | I either gave it to Pavla   |
| -- or    | Honzovi.                    | or to Honza.                |
+----------+-----------------------------+-----------------------------+
| **ani -- | Nebyl tam ani on ani ona.   | Neither he nor she were     |
| ani**    |                             | there.                      |
|          | Nemám čas ani dneska ani    |                             |
| neither  | zítra.                      | I won\'t have time neither  |
| -- nor   |                             | today nor tomorrow.         |
+----------+-----------------------------+-----------------------------+
| **nebo** | Uvidíme se v sobotu nebo v  | We\'ll see each other on    |
|          | neděli.                     | Sat. or Sun.                |
| or       |                             |                             |
|          | Chci modrý nebo červený     | I want a blue sweater or    |
|          | svetr.                      | red sweater.                |
+----------+-----------------------------+-----------------------------+

##### Causal

+:------------+:----------------------------+:----------------------------+
| **proto**   | Moc kouří, proto je často   | He smokes a lot that\'s why |
|             | nervózní.                   | he is often nervous.        |
| that\'s     |                             |                             |
| why,        | Jdu do kina a proto nemám   | I\'m going to the movies,   |
|             | čas.                        | that\'s why I don\'t have   |
|             |                             | time.                       |
+-------------+-----------------------------+-----------------------------+
| **protože** | Protože jsem přišel pozdě,  | Because I came late, I      |
|             | ujel mi vlak.               | missed my train.            |
| because     |                             |                             |
+-------------+-----------------------------+-----------------------------+

##### Relative

+:----------+:----------------------------+:----------------------------+
| **kdo**   | Vím, kdo mluví německy.     | I know who speaks German.   |
|           |                             |                             |
| who       |                             |                             |
+-----------+-----------------------------+-----------------------------+
| **co**    | Vím, co chceš.              | I know what you want.       |
|           |                             |                             |
| what      |                             |                             |
+-----------+-----------------------------+-----------------------------+
| **kdy**   | Nevím, kdy přijde.          | I don\'t know when he\'ll   |
|           |                             | come.                       |
| when      |                             |                             |
+-----------+-----------------------------+-----------------------------+
| **kam**   | Nevíš, kam šel?             | Do you know where he went   |
|           |                             | to?                         |
| where to  |                             |                             |
+-----------+-----------------------------+-----------------------------+
| **kde**   | Nevíš, kde je?              | Do you now where she is?    |
|           |                             |                             |
| where     |                             |                             |
+-----------+-----------------------------+-----------------------------+
| **jak**   | Řekl mi, jak to mám udělat. | He told me how to do it.    |
|           |                             |                             |
| how       |                             |                             |
+-----------+-----------------------------+-----------------------------+
| **který** | Viděl jsem film, který      | I saw the film which Forman |
|           | natočil Forman.             | made.                       |
| what,     |                             |                             |
| which     |                             |                             |
+-----------+-----------------------------+-----------------------------+
| **než**   | Máš se lépe než já.         | You are better than me.     |
|           |                             |                             |
| than      |                             |                             |
+-----------+-----------------------------+-----------------------------+

##### Time

+:------------+:----------------------------+:----------------------------+
| **když**    | Když jsem byl v Praze,      | When I was in Prague I      |

| when        |                             |                             |

|             | knihu.                      | buy a book.                 |

|             |                             |                             |

|             | domů.                       | will go home.               |
+-------------+-----------------------------+-----------------------------+
| **než**     | Dříve než ti odpovím, musím | Before I answer I have to   |

| before      |                             |                             |

#### Selected Problematic Conjunctions

##### AŽ *and* KDYŽ

Both až and když can be used in more than one context (see table below).
Only when used as the equivalent of the English when (i.e. temporally)
can they become a source of confusion for the learner. Až always points
to the future, while když describes events in the past, or the present,
most often repeatedly (for instance, ***Vždycky když \...*** . is the
only way to translate ***always when***\...).

+---------------+-----------------+-----------------+----------------+----------------+
|               | **až**                            | **když**                        |
+---------------+-----------------+-----------------+----------------+----------------+
|               | až přijdu       | when I come     | když přišel    | when he came   |
+---------------+-----------------+-----------------+----------------+----------------+
| **temporal**  | až to bude      | when it\'s      | když to bylo   | when it was    |
|               | hotové          | ready           | hotové         | ready          |
+---------------+-----------------+-----------------+----------------+----------------+
|               | až pojedeš do   | when you (will) | když jedeš do  | when you go to |
|               | Brna            | go to Brno      | Brna           | Brno           |
+---------------+-----------------+-----------------+----------------+----------------+
| **local**     | až do Prahy     | all the way to  |                |                |
|               |                 | Prague          |                |                |
+---------------+-----------------+-----------------+----------------+----------------+
|               | až v Praze      | not until       |                |                |
|               |                 | Prague          |                |                |
+---------------+-----------------+-----------------+----------------+----------------+
| **condition** |                 |                 | když přijdu    | if I come      |
+---------------+-----------------+-----------------+----------------+----------------+
|               |                 |                 | když to udělá  | if he does it  |
+---------------+-----------------+-----------------+----------------+----------------+

Both až and když are found in a number of idioms and proverbs. E.g.,
***když už tak už*** (used as when we\'re doing it anyway it might
better be worth it), ***až na to \...*** (except that \...), ***mám toho
až až***, (I\'m fed up with it), ***to je až moc*** (it\'s a little bit
too much). There\'s even ***až když*** which means ***only when***.
***Když*** + future tense is the equivalent of English 1^st^
conditional.

##### KDY *and* KDYŽ

Now it's time to put ***když*** into opposition with ***kdy***. The
basic difference is similar to that of between který and co. They both
mean when. But the basic usage of ***kdy*** is related to time, e.g.
question word as in ***Kdy příjdeš?*** (When will you come?), or
conjunction as in ***Řekněte mi, kdy se můžeme sejít.*** (Tell me when
we can meet.) while ***když*** is basically used as a conjunction in
relative clauses ***Když přišel, bylo to hotové.*** (When he came, it
was done.)

+------------------+--------------------+-------------------+-------------------+
| Temporal                              | Relative                              |
+------------------+--------------------+-------------------+-------------------+
| Kdy přijdeš?     | When will you      | Když přijdeš,     | When you come it  |
|                  | come?              | bude to hotové.   | will be done.     |
+------------------+--------------------+-------------------+-------------------+
| Až to bude       | [When]{.underline} | Když to bude      | If it's done you  |
| hotové dostaneš  | it's done you will | hotové, dostaneš  | will get money.   |
| peníze.          | get money.         | peníze.           |                   |
+------------------+--------------------+-------------------+-------------------+

***Když*** is also used in the following way. ***Čtu jen když je
světlo.*** (I read only when there is light.) ***Když to jinak nejde,
přijdu.*** (If that's the only way, I will come.) ***Když se mě to
nelíbí, tak si to nekoupím.*** (If I don't like it, I won't buy it.)
***Když přijde, všechno bude v pořádku.*** (If/when she comes,
everything will be OK.) ***Co když se jim něco stane?*** (What if
something happens to them?) ***Udělám to [i když]{.underline} nemám
čas.*** (I will do it [even though]{.underline} I don't have time.)
***Když ono je to tak hezké.*** (But it is so nice!).

***Kdy*** is also used in a few rather bookish idioms. ***Nemám kdy.***
(I don't have time.) ***Na to není kdy.*** (There's not time for that.)
***Bylo to horší než kdy dříve.*** (It was worse than ever before.)

##### KTERÝ and ŽE

One that sometimes causes misunderstandings is the English conjunction
***that*** which actually serves a double purpose. Consider these
English sentences: 1) *This is the man that I saw.* 2) a. *He said that
he would do it. /* b. *He doesn\'t know that he owes me money. **That***
in 1) further describes a person (or introduces a relative clause) while
in 2/ it indicates a. reported speech and b. a reported fact. These two
are easily distinguished, and in Czech they require a different word. 1)
in Czech, would be *To je ten muž, kterého jsem viděl.* while 2) will be
a. *Řekl, že to udělá.* b. *Neví, že mi dluží peníze. **Který*** is
really an adjective, and therefore it takes on the case of the noun in
the main clause (in Czech grammars you will find it classified as a
*relative pronoun*). ***Že***, on the other hand, is a true conjunction,
and it doesn\'t change at all. **Note:** Unlike ***that*** in English,
both ***který*** and ***že*** are always preceded by a comma.

##### AŤ and ABY

The dreaded *aby* and *ať* are next. The indirect imperatives, or
subjunctives. They are very similar to French subjunctives. ***Ať*** is
followed by the he, she, or they form of the verb, while ***aby***
requires the past tense. It takes on the function of the auxiliary, and
changes its form to *abych, abys, aby, abysme, abyste, aby* (in literary
Czech the correct we-form is abychom). The difference in meaning between
ať and aby is very slight . The sentences in the chart below should
demonstrate.

+------------------+------------------+-------------------+-------------------+
| AŤ (direct wish)                    | ABY (indirect wish)                   |
+------------------+------------------+-------------------+-------------------+
| Řekni mu, ať     | Tell him to      | Řekni mu, aby     | Tell him to come! |
| přijde!          | come.            | přišel!           |                   |
+------------------+------------------+-------------------+-------------------+
| Ať si jde.       | Let him go.      | Chci, aby šel.    | I want him to go. |
+------------------+------------------+-------------------+-------------------+
|                  |                  | Chce, abychom tam | He wants us to go |
|                  |                  | šli.              | there.            |
+------------------+------------------+-------------------+-------------------+
|                  |                  | Nechci, aby byla  | I don\'t want     |
|                  |                  | válka.            | there to be war.  |
+------------------+------------------+-------------------+-------------------+
|                  |                  | Aby přijel, musí  | He must have      |
|                  |                  | mít peníze.       | money in order to |
|                  |                  |                   | come.             |
+------------------+------------------+-------------------+-------------------+

**\*** Outside this system lie Conjunctions (and, if, or), Interjections
(ouch!) and Particles (thus) which never change their form or influence
other words to change form. **Note:** Some pronouns function as
conjunctions (who, which, whose -- so called relative pronouns) and of
course change their form with all the categories relevant to pronouns.

**\*\*** Masculine nouns and adjectives can be either animate or
inanimate thus in effect multiplying the number of genders to four.

Other parts of speech (Conjunctions, Interjections, Particles) work
independently as part of the sentence as a whole.

Blank cells indicate a word order not found in Czech (e.g. Prepositions
never follow any other part of speech. Or adverbs don't follow
prepositions in a way that modifies the adverb. But adverbs may precede
an adjective in a phrase that is preceded by a preposition as in, "in a
very long book".

## Word Order

Contrary to popular belief, Czech word order is not completely free. In
Czech, word order serves to show what you want to stress. The general
rule is *What you want to stress goes at the end of the sentence.* For
instance, take the simplest possible sentence *Petr pije kolu.* (Petr is
drinking coke.) The words in this sentence can be ordered in any way and
it will still mean the same (unlike English where *Coke is drinking
Peter* is a little awkward). BUT. Each of the orderings serves as an
answer to a different question.

  ----------------------------------- -----------------------------------
  **Question**                        **Answer**

  **Co** pije Petr? (What is Petr     Petr pije **kolu**.
  drinking?)                          

  **Kdo** pije kolu? (Who is drinking Kolu pije **Petr**.
  coke?)                              
  ----------------------------------- -----------------------------------

Therefore, unlike English, from even a very simple answer you can guess
what the question was. Notice that the answers are mirror images of
their questions.

**Note:** As you may see below the other possible combinations also make
sense in Czech

+:---------------------------+:-----------------------------------+
| Jí kolu Petr?              | *Petr kolu pije.* (No, Petr        |
|                            | doesn\'t eat coke he drinks it)    |
+----------------------------+------------------------------------+
| Jí Petr kolu?              | *Kolu Petr pije.*                  |
+----------------------------+------------------------------------+
|                            |                                    |
+----------------------------+------------------------------------+
| Pije Petr kolu? (not       | (these two combinations would be   |
| mléko, or čaj)             | most likely perceived as           |
|                            | questions)                         |
| Pije kolu Petr? (not Jana, |                                    |
| or Karel)                  |                                    |
+----------------------------+------------------------------------+

Three words, thus, give us six possible combinations, and all have a
slightly different meaning in Czech. The word order is always a little
vague for even in English you have sentences like *see it he did*, *Petr
he saw*, *Petr saw he*. To grasp the subtle differences you have to live
with the language for a long time.

### Typical Word Order of a Czech Sentence

##### Simple sentence

  ------------ ------------ ------------ ------------
  1            2            3            4

  subject      verb         indirect     object
                            object       

  Petr         píše         Martině      dopis.

  Petr         is writing   to Martina   a letter.
  ------------ ------------ ------------ ------------

**Note:** **3** and **4** are interchangeable.

##### Sentence with adverbials

+-----------+-----------+-----------+-----------+-----------+-----------+
| 1         | 2         | 3         | 4         | 5         | 6         |
+-----------+-----------+-----------+-----------+-----------+-----------+
| adverbial | verb      | subject   | indirect  | object    | adverbial |
|           |           |           | object    |           |           |
| of time   |           |           |           |           | of place  |
+-----------+-----------+-----------+-----------+-----------+-----------+
| Včera     | psal      | Petr      | Martině   | dopis     | v parku.  |
+-----------+-----------+-----------+-----------+-----------+-----------+
| Yesterday | was       | Petr      | to        | a letter  | in the    |
|           | writing   |           | Martina   |           | park.     |
+-----------+-----------+-----------+-----------+-----------+-----------+

+-------+------------------------------------------------------------------+
| **!** | - Any part of the sentence could be moved at the end to be       |
|       |   stressed:                                                      |
|       |                                                                  |
|       | <!-- -->                                                         |
|       |                                                                  |
|       | - Petr psal Martině dopis v parku **VČERA**. (not the day        |
|       |   before)                                                        |
|       |                                                                  |
|       | - Dopis psal včera v parku Martině **PETR**. (not Jirka)         |
|       |                                                                  |
|       | - Petr psal včera v parku Martině **DOPIS**. (not a poem)        |
|       |                                                                  |
|       | - Petr psal včera v parku dopis **MARTINĚ**. (not to Jana)       |
|       |                                                                  |
|       | - Petr včera dopis Martině v parku **PSAL**. (not read)          |
|       |                                                                  |
|       |   **Note:** The finite verb tends to keep **the second**         |
|       |   position. (if unstressed)                                      |
|       |                                                                  |
|       | <!-- -->                                                         |
|       |                                                                  |
|       | - The adverbial of time usually precedes that of space.\         |
|       |   Otherwise the position of adverbials is fairly flexible        |
|       |                                                                  |
|       | - si, se, mě, mi, ti, se, si, ho, mu (jsem, \..., jste in byl    |
|       |   jsem,\..., byli jste)                                          |
|       |                                                                  |
|       | <!-- -->                                                         |
|       |                                                                  |
|       | - always take the second position in the sentence                |
|       |                                                                  |
|       | - Měl jsem se ho zeptat. (I should have asked him.)              |
|       |                                                                  |
|       | <!-- -->                                                         |
|       |                                                                  |
|       | - adjectives always precede nouns - *dobrý učitel, malý muž,     |
|       |   žlutý banán*\                                                  |
|       |   (the reverse - French like - order is possible but would sound |
|       |   archaic)                                                       |
+-------+------------------------------------------------------------------+

### How does *se* change around in a sentence?

It in fact *se* doesn\'t move at all. *se* always occupies the second
position in the sentence, and other words move around it to allow for
that.

+---------+-----+---------+-------+--------+
| 1       | 2   | 3       | 4              |
+---------+-----+---------+-------+--------+
| Jak     | se  | jmenuješ?       |        |
+---------+-----+-----------------+--------+
| Jmenuju | se  | Petr.           |        |
+---------+-----+-----------------+--------+
| Jak     | se  | máš?            |        |
+---------+-----+-----------------+--------+
| Mám     | se  | dobře.          |        |
+---------+-----+-----------------+--------+
| Jak     | se  | má              | Petr?  |
+---------+-----+-----------------+--------+
| Petr    | se  | má              | dobře. |
+---------+-----+-----------------+--------+

### Where are *jsem* and *budu* placed in the past and future tense?

The auxiallry form of the verb *to be* (jsem, jsi, jsme, jste) in the
past tense is almost always in the second position.

  -------- ------ ---- ----------- -----------
  1        2      3    4           5

  Koupil   jsem   si   to          včera.

  Včera    jsem   si   to          koupil.

  Koupil   jsi    to   včera.      

  Díval    jsem   se   na          
                       televizi.   

  Tak      jsem   se   Díval       na
                                   televizi.
  -------- ------ ---- ----------- -----------

The auxiliary form of the verb *to be* (budu), can be anywhere in the
sentence depending on the emphasis on. For example:

- Zítra budu psát knihu. (Tomorrow, I will be writing a book).

- Budu zítra psát knihu? (Will I be writing a book tomorrow?)

- Zítra knihu psát budu. (I **will** \[definitely\] be writing a book
  tomorrow.)

- Zítra budu knihu psát. (Writing is what I will be doing with the book
  tomorrow).

### Questions and answers in Czech

##### co? kdo? kdy? kde? kam? proč?

+--------------+----------------+----------------+----------------+----------------+-------------------------+
| **Co** píše Petr?             |                |                |                | Petr píše **dopis**.    |
+-------------------------------+----------------+----------------+----------------+-------------------------+
| **Kdo** píše dopis?           |                |                |                | Dopis píše **Petr**.    |
+-------------------------------+----------------+----------------+----------------+-------------------------+
| **Kdy** čte Martina?          |                |                |                | Martina čte **večer**.  |
+-------------------------------+----------------+----------------+----------------+-------------------------+
| **Kde** se učili studenti?    |                |                |                | Studenti se učili **v   |
|                               |                |                |                | parku**.                |
+-------------------------------+----------------+----------------+----------------+-------------------------+
| **Kam** šly děti?             |                |                |                | Děti šly **do           |
|                               |                |                |                | školy**[.]{.underline}  |
+-------------------------------+----------------+----------------+----------------+-------------------------+
| **Proč** chcete jíst?         |                |                |                | Chceme jíst, **protože  |
|                               |                |                |                | \...** .                |
+--------------+----------------+----------------+----------------+----------------+-------------------------+
| !            | - if a question starts with a *question word* the word order of the answer mirrors          |
|              |                                                                                             |
|              | - the **intonation** of this kind of question is identical with that of simple statements   |
+--------------+---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------+

##### ano *nebo* ne

+--------------+--------------+--------------+--------------+--------------+--------------+-----------------------+
| **Píše** *Petr* dopis?      |              |              |              | Ano.         | *Petr* **píše**       |
|                             |              |              |              |              | dopis.                |
+-----------------------------+--------------+--------------+--------------+--------------+-----------------------+
| **Píše** *dopis* Petr?      |              |              |              | Ano.         | *Dopis* **píše**      |
|                             |              |              |              |              | Petr.                 |
+-----------------------------+--------------+--------------+--------------+--------------+-----------------------+
| **Čte** *Martina* večer?    |              |              |              | Ano.         | *Martina* **čte**     |
|                             |              |              |              |              | večer.                |
+-----------------------------+--------------+--------------+--------------+--------------+-----------------------+
| **Šly** *děti* do školy?    |              |              |              | Ano.         | *Děti* **šly** do     |
|                             |              |              |              |              | školy.                |
+-----------------------------+--------------+--------------+--------------+--------------+-----------------------+
| **Učili se** *studenti* v   |              |              |              | Ano.         | *Studenti* **se       |
| parku?                      |              |              |              |              | učili** v parku.      |
+-----------------------------+--------------+--------------+--------------+--------------+-----------------------+
| Chcete *jíst*?              |              |              |              | Ano.         | Chceme *jíst*.        |
+--------------+--------------+--------------+--------------+--------------+--------------+-----------------------+
| !            | - in *YES/NO questions* you typically swap the *verb* and the *noun*                             |
|              |                                                                                                  |
|              | - however, if your question requires a *YES* or *NO* answer, *ano* or *ne* will suffice          |
|              |                                                                                                  |
|              | - the word order of an answer to a *YES/NO question* is your typical word order for simple       |
|              |   statements                                                                                     |
|              |                                                                                                  |
|              | - the **intonation** of this type of question has a rising pattern - it goes up on the last      |
|              |   syllable in the utterance                                                                      |
+--------------+--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------+

### Negation

The negative particle **ne** behaves differently in different tenses.
Most of the time it is attached directly to the main verb. Only in the
**future with** **budu**, budu is negated instead.

  ----------------- --------------- ------------------ ---------------------
                    **Yes**         **No**             

  **Past**          dělal jsem to   **ne**dělal jsem   I wasn't doing it
                                    to                 

  **Present**       dělám to        **ne**dělám to     I'm not doing it

  **Conditional**   dělal bych to   **ne**dělal bych   I wouldn't do it
                                    to                 

  **Future**        budu to dělat   **ne**budu to      I'm not going to do
                                    dělat              it.
  ----------------- --------------- ------------------ ---------------------

**Note:** Sentences like **Ne**jsem to dělal. **Ne**bych jela. or Budu
to **ne**dělat. are wrong.

### Double Negatives

In Czech, double negatives are a requirement! All pronouns and adverbs
used to quantify have to be used in the negative form in addition to the
verb in negative form.

  ------------------ ------------------ ----------------------------------
  **ANO**            **NE**             

  Mám všechno.       **Ne**mám **nic**. I have everything / nothing.

  Ví všechno.        Neví nic.          She knows everything / nothing.

  Mám nějaký rohlík. **Ne**mám          I have some bread / no bread.
                     **žádný** rohlík.  

  Příjde vždycky     **Ne**přijde       He always comes... / He never
  ráno.              **nikdy**.         comes.
  ------------------ ------------------ ----------------------------------

### Reference Pronouns (Anaphors)

No language likes to repeat nouns over and over. Instead, pronouns are
used to refer back to nouns mentioned before. For example, instead of
saying *He bought a new car. The car was expensive.* you say *He bought
a new car. **It** was expensive.* You can also refer back to verb or
whole sentences. For example, *I ran a mile under 10 minutes. **It***
*was very difficult.*

English uses many pronouns for this purpose: *he, she, it, him, her,
his*

There are several things to remember about Czech in this respect.

- The pronoun has to match the gender.

- The pronoun may change according to case, especially after
  prepositions.

- The pronoun doesn't always have to be expressed.

  ----------- --------------- ---------------------- ---------------------
  **Nouns**   unexpressed     Petr má nové kolo.\    Petr has a new bike.\
              pronoun         Je červené.            **It** is read.

              on, ona         Jana si koupila knihu, Jana bought a book
                              ale\                   but\
                              **ona** nebyla dobrá.  **it** wasn't good.

              mě, jemu, ji,   Petra nepřinesla       Petra didn't bring a
              jí              tužku,\                pen\
                              protože **ji** nemohla because she couldn't
                              najít.                 find **it**.

              s ním, bez něho Mám dobrý počítač,\    I have a good
                              bez **něho** nemůžu    computer,\
                              pracovat.              without **it** I
                                                     cannot work.

  **Verbs**   to              Včera jsem ujel 100    Yesterday, I drove
                              km,\                   100 km, **it** was
                              bylo **to** těžké.     difficult.

              s tím           Nemám peníze.\         I have no money.
                              S **tím** se nedá nic  There's nothing to be
                              dělat.                 done about **it**.
  ----------- --------------- ---------------------- ---------------------

### Relative Clauses and Phrases

Relative clause or phrase is always begun with a relative particle. See
*Conjunctions* for a list. The important thing to remember about the
relative conjunctions (pronouns) *který* (which)*, kdo* (who)*, jaký*
(what) is that they change with the case and gender of the noun they
refer to. *Co* (what) can be used with any case without change.

#### Který

*Který* can be translated into English as *who, what, that, which*. More
often than not, English drops the relative pronoun, Czech never does.
*Který* is always preceded by a comma.

  ------------- ----------------------------- ---------------------------
  **Kdo? Co?**  muž, **který** přišel,\       man who came,\
                auto, **které** nejede        car that doesn't run

  **Koho? Co?** muž, **kterého** jsem viděl,\ the man I saw\
                žena, **kterou** znám\        the woman I know\
                auto, **které** jsem koupil   the car I bought

  **Koho?       muž, **kterého** jsem se      the man I asked\
  Čeho?**       zeptal\                       city in which I arrived
                město, do **kterého** jsem    
                přijel                        

  **Komu?       kamarád, **kterému** jsem     a friend I called\
  Čemu?**       telefonoval\                  woman who I told it to
                žena, **které** jsem to řekl  

  **Kom? Čem?** muž, o **kterém** jsme        man about whom we talked\
                mluvili\                      country in which there is
                země, ve **které** je svoboda freedom

  **Kým? Čím?** člověk, se **kterým** se      man who I know\
                znám\                         woman with who I came
                žena, se **kterou** přišel    
  ------------- ----------------------------- ---------------------------

#### Kdo

*Kdo* (who) is used differently from English. It is common only in set
phrases and only for *TEN* nouns. *Kdo* is always preceded by a comma.
For other genders, *který* or *co* is used.

  --------------- ---------------------------- ---------------------------
  **Kdo? Co?**    ten, kdo přišel              he who came

  **Koho? Co?**   ten, koho znám               he who I know

  **Komu? Čemu?** ten, komu to dám             he who I give it to

  **Kým? Čím?**   ten, s kým přijdeš           he with whom you come
  --------------- ---------------------------- ---------------------------

#### Co

In colloquial Czech, you can use *co* (what) in any relative clause
regardless of gender or case. The object must also be expressed again by
a pronoun in all transitive verbs where in cases other than Kdo? Co?.

  ------------- --------------------------- -----------------------------
  **Kdo? Co?**  muž, **co** přišel,\        man who came,\
                auto, **co** nejede         car that doesn't run

  **Koho? Co?** muž, **co** jsem **ho**     the man I saw\
                viděl,\                     the woman I know
                žena, **co ji** znám        

  **Koho?       muž, **co** jsem se **ho**  the man I asked\
  Čeho?**       zeptal\                     city in which I arrived
                město, **co** jsem do       
                **něj** přijel              

  **Komu?       kamarád, **co** jsem **mu** a friend I called\
  Čemu?**       telefonoval\                woman who I told it to
                žena, **co** jsem **jí** to 
                řekl                        

  **Kom? Čem?** muž, **co** jsme **o něm**  man about who we talked\
                mluvili\                    country in which there is
                země, **co** **v ní** je    freedom
                svoboda                     

  **Kým? Čím?** člověk, **co** se **s ním** man who I know\
                znám\                       woman who I came with
                paní, **co** jsem s **ní**  
                přišel                      
  ------------- --------------------------- -----------------------------

### Sentences without Subject

See how these are used as idioms under *Idioms, Phraseology and
Vocabulary.*

#### Weather - impersonal states

  ------------------ ------------------------ ---------------------------
  Prší.              It\'s raining.           Bude pršet. Pršelo.

  Sněží.             It\'s snowing.           Bude sněžit. Sněžilo.

  Mrzne.             It\'s freezing.          Bude mrznout. Mrzlo.

  Hřmí.              There\'s thunder.        Bude hřmít. Hřmělo.

  Blýská se.         There\'s lightning.      Bude se blýskat. Blýskalo
                                              se.

  Taje.              There\'s a thaw.         Bude tát. Tálo.
  ------------------ ------------------------ ---------------------------

#### *je* + adverb 

This construction is used most often for describing states of weather,
time, *etc.*

  ------------------ ------------------------ ---------------------------
  Jak je venku?      How\'s the weather       Jak bude venku? Jak bylo
                     outside?                 venku?

  Je teplo.          It is warm.              Bude teplo. Bylo teplo.

  Je horko.          It is hot.               Bude horko. Bylo horko.

  Je hezky.          It is nice.              Bude hezky. Bylo hezky.

  Je chladno.        It is cold.              Bude chladno. Bylo chladno.

  Je zima.           It is cold.              Bude zima. Byla zima.

  Je pět hodin.      It\'s five o\'clock.     Bude pět hodin. Bylo pět
                                              hodin.
  ------------------ ------------------------ ---------------------------

#### Feelings - *je* + *Komu? Čemu?* + adverb

  ------------------ ----------------------- ----------------------------
  Jak je ti?         How are you feeling?    Jak ti bude? Jak ti bylo?

  Je mi dobře.       I\'m feeling well.      Bude mi dobře.Bylo mi dobře.

  Je mi zima.        I\'m cold.              Bude mi zima. Bylo mi zima.

  Je mi špatně.      I\'m feeling sick.      Bude mi špatně. Bylo mi
                                             špatně.

  Je mi teplo.       I\'m hot.               Bude mi teplo. Bylo mi
                                             teplo.

  Je mi smutno.      I\'m feeling sad.       Bude mi smutno. Bylo mi
                                             smutno.
  ------------------ ----------------------- ----------------------------

## Pronunciation

### Pronunciation of individual sounds

Czech prounciation is not particularly difficult. It is about as hard as
it is to pronounce any language, if it does not happen to be the one you
grew up with. This is because your muscles just find it hard to adjust
to new things. It is true, however, that pronunciation is often the most
difficult part for adult learners and adults very rarely achieve
native-like accent.

It is still important to practice pronunciation when learning Czech
because Czech native speakers tend to value correct pronunciation over
grammar when interacting with non-native speakers. A student of Czech
will have an easier time trying to converse with natives speakers when
getting the basics of pronunciation right.

#### Vowels

There are a few peculiarities in Czech that tend to cause problems for
English-speaking mouths. The most troublesome is the pronunciation of
**vowels**.

1\) no matter where they are in the word, Czech vowels never loose their
full quality, and are always **pronounced fully** and unflaggingly.

2\) each Czech vowel can be either **short or long**. Long vowels are
exactly two times longer than their short brothers. It is very frequent
that vowel length changes meaning of words significantly. For instance,
*děl[a]{.underline}* (cannons) is differentiated from
*děl[á]{.underline}* (s/he is working) only by the long *á* in the
latter, and there are even more embarrassing examples. The length of
vowels is completely independent on the **stress**. The stress is always
on the first syllable in all Czech words. It is a very frequent that a
long vowel occurs in an unstressed position as in
***dě**l[á]{.underline}*. There are two diphthongs *ou, au* and *eu*
that are pronounced fully, as well.

#### Consonants

Czech **consonants** are much less awkward. There are many that are the
same as or very similar to those in English. There are five, however,
that are not in English at all: *ch, ď, ť, ň, **ř***

- of the above **ř** is the most feared one; it is famous for its quirky
  nature and soft gurgling sound; it is the sound that Czech children
  learn last (around 5 -- 6 years), and a certain portion of the
  population, including former President Havel, never learn to do it
  quite right; except for its fame and difficulty (it has been claimed
  that Czechs invented it deliberately to make Czech harder to learn)
  **ř** has little importance for learning the language, and many Czechs
  will be disappointed if you learn it well, which you very likely will

- **ch** is the same as in Scottish loch but mispronouncing it will
  cause no disasterous misunderstandings;

- **ď**, **ť**, **ň**, on the other hand have no claims to fame but play
  a much more important role in the language because they often
  distinguish between different meanings of similar words

There are even more issues with sounds which are in both English and
Czech but are pronounced just a little differently in Czech. For
instance, *k, p, t* don\'t have aspiration, so it will be difficult for
you to distinguish them from *g, b, d* when they are the first sound in
a word. Czech *r*\'s are rolled very much like the Scottish *r*\'s.
Finally, unlike English, Czech *h* is voiced, which probably makes it
the most difficult of Czech sounds for English speakers. Other than
that, there\'s nothing in Czech that will surprise you.

#### Contrasts

There are several groups of sounds which have something in common one
way or another. *Vowels* and *consonants* are one example. Vowels are
*long* and *short*. Consonants are *soft* and *hard*, or *voiced* and
*unvoiced*. It is important to be aware of this opposition since very
often when the form of a word changes the ending consonant changes from
hard to soft. See the *how to* spell it charts below for examples.

##### Short and long vowels

There are many pairs of Czech words which are distinguished only by the
length of the vowels.

##### Soft, hard and neutral consonants

This distinction is important for the choice of **i** or **y** which are
pronounced identically but spelled depending on the preceding consonant.

  ---------- ----- ------ ------ ------ ------ ------ ------ ------ --- --------
  **hard**   d     t      n      ch     k      h      g      r          \[-y\]

  **soft**   ď     ť      ň      š      c, č   z      ž      ř      j   \[- i\]
  ---------- ----- ------ ------ ------ ------ ------ ------ ------ --- --------

The distinction between soft and hard consonants is very important for
Czech.

One of the less important implications is the spelling of *i* and *y*
which are pronounced the same. The *i* only follows soft consonants, and
*y* hard.

But models of nouns and adjectives are also divided into hard and soft
depending on the final consontant. Soft and hard models have different
endings.

There is also a group of sounds that are neutral. Neither soft nor hard.

  ------------- ------ ------ ------ ------ ------ ------ ------ -------------
  **neutral**   b      f      l      m      p      s      v      \[- i
                                                                 \]/\[-y\]

  ------------- ------ ------ ------ ------ ------ ------ ------ -------------

Czechs have to learn by heart which of the neutral consonants are
followed by **i** or **y**. The default is **i** with a list of
exception that has to be memorised by every Czech school child.

##### Voiced vs unvoiced (voiceless)

Some consonants are *voiced* and some are *unvoiced* (or *voiceless*).
In Czech, it is very important to be aware of the difference because all
*voiced* consonants become *unvoiced* at the end of words. For instance,
*muž* (man) is pronounced [muš]{.underline}. The other rule is that
there can only be two consonants of the same quality next to each other,
and the second governs the first. For instance, *kdy* (when) is
pronounced [gdy]{.underline}, etc.

  -------------- --- ------ ---- ---- --- ---- --- ---- ----- ---- -----
  **voiced**     b   v      d    ď    g   h    z   ž    dž    dz   ř

  **unvoiced**   p   f      t    ť    k   ch   s   š    č     c    *ř*
  -------------- --- ------ ---- ---- --- ---- --- ---- ----- ---- -----

The following consonants do not have unvoiced counterparts, and
therefore are not influenced by nor do they do anything to other
consonants: *r, l, m, n, j*. So e.g. *z* in *znát* (to know) does not
change to *s* as it would for instance in *zpátky* (back). Nor does *s*
followed by *n* change to *z* as in *sníst* (to eat up) as it would in
*les dole* (forest below) pronounced as *lezdole*. See section on
Assimilation below for much more detail.

**Note:** The last three contrasts only appear in cases of assimilation.
The exception is the the loan word *džus* which forms a minimal pair
with another loan word *čus*. There are other rare loan words where *dž*
appears unassimilated such as *maharádža* but which do not form minimal
pairs with other words.

**Note 2:** *Ř* becomes voiceless when next to another voiceless
consonant, e.g. it is pronounced voiceless in *tři* but voiced in
*čtyři*.
